1	# Bugs
2	
3	This file uses [Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form). It
4	lists known defects we're aware of but haven't scheduled a fix for.
5	Each entry describes what goes wrong, when, and the cost of
6	the failure. Entries are numbered (`1.` `2.` …) the same way
7	as `## Todo` in `todo.md`; run
8	`vc-x1 fix-todo --no-dry-run notes/bugs.md` to renumber after
9	insert / delete / reorder.
10	
11	## Bugs
12	
13	1. `reserve_slot` (both `Consumer` and `Producer`) is a strictly
14	   dominated API rung — `reserve_slot_with` does everything it does,
15	   never worse and sometimes better — and its naive "loop on the
16	   fallible call" usage carries a measurable latency + jitter cost.
17	   Consider removing both.
18	   - **What we measured.** iiac-perf's zcr round-trip benches
19	     (`-d 60`, unpinned 3900X, one message in flight) compare the
20	     tiers under real cross-core traffic. `zcr-raw-2t` (main loops on
21	     `reserve_slot` while waiting for the reply) lands a body mean
22	     ~130 ns, multimodal across ~120–150 ns, trimmed `min..n2` stdev
23	     ~15 ns. `zcr-with-2t` / `zcr-spin-2t` (same wait via
24	     `reserve_slot_with` / `reserve_slot_spin`) land ~110 ns, body
25	     clustered at 100/109/110 ns, trimmed stdev ~7 ns. So the raw
26	     retry pattern costs ~+30 ns and ~2× the body jitter. `with` and
27	     `spin` are statistically identical, as expected —
28	     `reserve_slot_spin` *is* `reserve_slot_with(policy::spin)`.
29	   - **Why (we think).** The only code difference on the wait path is
30	     that `reserve_slot`-in-a-loop re-reads the caller-*owned* index
31	     (`consumer_idx` on the read side, `producer_idx` on the write
32	     side) on every iteration, because each call reloads it;
33	     `reserve_slot_with` loads the owned index once and re-reads only
34	     the peer's index per attempt (its doc already states this).
35	     A relaxed atomic load can't be hoisted/coalesced out of a loop
36	     by LLVM, so the naive retry genuinely issues the extra load each
37	     spin. We think that lengthens and de-tightens the spin, so it
38	     samples the peer's store later and less regularly — a higher
39	     mean and a multimodal body in ~10 ns steps (≈ one Zen 2
40	     cross-core coherence hop). The two indices are each
41	     `CacheAligned` on separate lines, so this is **not** false
42	     sharing between them.
43	   - **Current thinking.** `reserve_slot` ≡
44	     `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)` (give up after the first failed
45	     attempt) — identical loads, identical result, and the `|_| false`
46	     loop folds away (worth a disasm confirm). For any retry beyond
47	     one probe, `_with` hoists the owned load and wins. The desirable
48	     always-non-blocking mode (e.g. driving the ring from a Tokio
49	     task) is `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)`; the better hybrid
50	     ("spin a bounded N, then return pending and let the runtime
51	     park") is `reserve_slot_with(|attempt| attempt < N)` — both
52	     things `reserve_slot` can't express. So the proposed shape is a
53	     two-rung ladder per endpoint: `reserve_slot_with` (fallible
54	     primitive) + `reserve_slot_spin` (forever-spin convenience),
55	     dropping `reserve_slot`. If a real consumer later makes
56	     `_with(|_| false)` common, add a thin `try_reserve_slot` alias
57	     (named like `Sender::try_send`) then — not before (no consumers
58	     exist yet).
59	   - **Downstream.** iiac-perf's `zcr-raw-1t` / `zcr-raw-2t` call
60	     `reserve_slot` directly and only exist to measure this naive
61	     pattern; removing the method drops (or relabels) those benches.
62	     Tracked on the iiac-perf side.
63	
64	# References
65

1	//! Consuming endpoint: [`Consumer`] reserves the oldest unread
2	//! slot, the [`ReadSlot`] guard reads it in place and releases.
3	
4	use core::marker::PhantomData;
5	use core::ops::Deref;
6	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
7	use zerocopy::{FromBytes, Immutable, KnownLayout};
8	
9	use crate::{Header, USER_WORDS, check_type, slot_ptr};
10	
11	/// `reserve_slot` failed: no unread messages.
12	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
13	pub struct Empty;
14	
15	/// The consuming endpoint: `reserve_slot` the oldest unread
16	/// slot, read in place, `release`.
17	pub struct Consumer<'a> {
18	    /// The ring's control block.
19	    header: &'a Header,
20	    /// Base of the slot array.
21	    slots: *mut u8,
22	    /// Geometry snapshot (see [`Ring`](crate::Ring)).
23	    slot_size: u32,
24	    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).
25	    mask: u32,
26	    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,
27	}
28	
29	// SAFETY: the handle owns the consumer role; see the Producer
30	// Send rationale.
31	unsafe impl Send for Consumer<'_> {}
32	
33	impl<'a> Consumer<'a> {
34	    /// Build the handle from [`Ring::split`](crate::Ring::split)'s
35	    /// geometry snapshot.
36	    pub(crate) fn new(header: &'a Header, slots: *mut u8, slot_size: u32, mask: u32) -> Self {
37	        Consumer {
38	            header,
39	            slots,
40	            slot_size,
41	            mask,
42	            _region: PhantomData,
43	        }
44	    }
45	
46	    /// The header's app-owned scratch line: zeroed at init,
47	    /// never touched by the crate again.
48	    ///
49	    /// - Shared with the peer: treat contents as untrusted
50	    ///   data — store values, never addresses to dereference.
51	    /// - See the design doc's "Blocking and user words" for
52	    ///   the wakeup-protocol contract it exists to host.
53	    pub fn user(&self) -> &[AtomicU32; USER_WORDS] {
54	        &self.header.user
55	    }
56	
57	    /// Reserve the oldest unread slot as a `&T`, or [`Empty`].
58	    ///
59	    /// - Only one slot may be reserved at a time: the guard
60	    ///   holds the `&mut Consumer` borrow, so a second
61	    ///   `reserve_slot` before the guard is dropped or released
62	    ///   does not compile.
63	    /// - Dropping the guard without [`ReadSlot::release`]
64	    ///   leaves the slot unread — the next `reserve_slot`
65	    ///   returns it again.
66	    pub fn reserve_slot<T>(&mut self) -> Result<ReadSlot<'_, T>, Empty>
67	    where
68	        T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable,
69	    {
70	        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);
71	        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
72	        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);
73	        if p == c {
74	            return Err(Empty);
75	        }
76	        // Raw pointer, not `&T`, for the same reason as in the
77	        // producer's reserve_slot: release's store frees the
78	        // slot for producer writes while a reference field
79	        // would still be argument-protected.
80	        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, c, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *const T;
81	        Ok(ReadSlot {
82	            header: self.header,
83	            msg,
84	            next_idx: c.wrapping_add(1),
85	            _slot: PhantomData,
86	        })
87	    }
88	
89	    /// [`reserve_slot`](Consumer::reserve_slot) with an
90	    /// injected wait policy: retry until a message arrives or
91	    /// the policy gives up.
92	    ///
93	    /// - `on_empty` is called after each failed attempt with
94	    ///   the attempt count (0-based, saturating); returning
95	    ///   `false` gives up → `Err(Empty)`.
96	    /// - The wait loop is the reservation itself (the guard
97	    ///   borrows the endpoint, so retrying a `reserve_slot`
98	    ///   call could not return it): `consumer_idx` is ours
99	    ///   and loaded once; only `producer_idx` is re-read per
100	    ///   attempt, so the no-wait fast path does exactly the
101	    ///   loads `reserve_slot` does.
102	    /// - See [`policy`](crate::policy) for shipped policies
103	    ///   and the composition model.
104	    pub fn reserve_slot_with<T>(
105	        &mut self,
106	        mut on_empty: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool,
107	    ) -> Result<ReadSlot<'_, T>, Empty>
108	    where
109	        T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable,
110	    {
111	        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);
112	        // Ours alone: no peer moves it, load once.
113	        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
114	        let mut attempt = 0u32;
115	        loop {
116	            let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);
117	            if p != c {
118	                break;
119	            }
120	            if !on_empty(attempt) {
121	                return Err(Empty);
122	            }
123	            attempt = attempt.saturating_add(1);
124	        }
125	        // Raw pointer, not `&T` — see reserve_slot.
126	        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, c, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *const T;
127	        Ok(ReadSlot {
128	            header: self.header,
129	            msg,
130	            next_idx: c.wrapping_add(1),
131	            _slot: PhantomData,
132	        })
133	    }
134	
135	    /// [`reserve_slot`](Consumer::reserve_slot), spinning
136	    /// until a message arrives —
137	    /// [`reserve_slot_with`](Consumer::reserve_slot_with)
138	    /// under the never-quitting
139	    /// [`policy::spin`](crate::policy::spin), so no `Result`.
140	    pub fn reserve_slot_spin<T>(&mut self) -> ReadSlot<'_, T>
141	    where
142	        T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable,
143	    {
144	        match self.reserve_slot_with(crate::policy::spin) {
145	            Ok(slot) => slot,
146	            Err(Empty) => unreachable!("policy::spin never gives up"),
147	        }
148	    }
149	}
150	
151	/// A reserved read slot: `Deref` to read the message, then
152	/// [`release`](ReadSlot::release).
153	pub struct ReadSlot<'c, T> {
154	    /// The ring's control block (for the release store).
155	    header: &'c Header,
156	    /// The slot, viewed as the message type. Raw on purpose —
157	    /// see the comment in [`Consumer::reserve_slot`].
158	    msg: *const T,
159	    /// Value `consumer_idx` takes on release.
160	    next_idx: u32,
161	    /// Owns the `&'c` borrow of the consumer.
162	    _slot: PhantomData<&'c T>,
163	}
164	
165	impl<T> Deref for ReadSlot<'_, T> {
166	    type Target = T;
167	    /// Read access to the in-slot message.
168	    fn deref(&self) -> &T {
169	        // SAFETY: msg is in-bounds and aligned (check_type +
170	        // cache-line slot base), any byte pattern is a valid T
171	        // (FromBytes bound at reserve_slot), and the index
172	        // protocol gives this guard read access until release.
173	        unsafe { &*self.msg }
174	    }
175	}
176	
177	impl<T> ReadSlot<'_, T> {
178	    /// Free the slot for reuse (`consumer_idx + 1`, `Release`).
179	    pub fn release(self) {
180	        self.header
181	            .consumer_idx
182	            .store(self.next_idx, Ordering::Release);
183	    }
184	}
185	
```

214	
215	/// Move COUNT messages producer-thread → consumer-thread
216	/// through the ring; return elapsed seconds.
217	///
218	/// - `pin` — `Some((p, c))` pins the producer to cpu `p` and
219	///   the consumer to cpu `c`; `None` lets the scheduler place
220	///   them (the number then depends on where they land).
221	fn spsc_ring_one_msg_2t(pin: PinPair) -> f64 {
222	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
223	    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)
224	        .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots
225	        .split();
226	
227	    let start = Instant::now();
228	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
229	        s.spawn(move || {
230	            if let Some((p, _)) = pin {
231	                pin_to_cpu(p);
232	            }
233	            for i in 0..COUNT {
234	                loop {
235	                    match producer.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
236	                        Ok(mut slot) => {
237	                            slot.seq = i;
238	                            slot.commit();
239	                            break;
240	                        }
241	                        Err(Full) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
242	                    }
243	                }
244	            }
245	        });
246	        s.spawn(move || {
247	            if let Some((_, c)) = pin {
248	                pin_to_cpu(c);
249	            }
250	            for i in 0..COUNT {
251	                loop {
252	                    match consumer.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
253	                        Ok(msg) => {
254	                            assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
255	                            msg.release();
256	                            break;
257	                        }
258	                        Err(Empty) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
259	                    }
260	                }
261	            }
262	        });
263	    });
264	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()

439	        s.spawn(move || {
440	            pin_to_cpu(0);
441	            let mut buf_slot = pool.alloc::<Msg>().unwrap(); // OK: fresh pool, DEPTH buffers free
442	            for i in 0..COUNT {
443	                buf_slot.seq = i;
444	                let desc = registry
445	                    .into_desc(pool_id, buf_slot)
446	                    .map_err(|(_, e)| e)
447	                    .unwrap(); // OK: pool_id came from this registry's register
448	                match producer.reserve_slot::<Desc>() {
449	                    Ok(mut slot) => {
450	                        *slot = desc;
451	                        slot.commit();
452	                    }
453	                    Err(Full) => {
454	                        panic!("spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_1t: producer Full SHOULD NOT HAPPEN");
455	                    }
456	                }
457	                buf_slot = match consumer.reserve_slot::<Desc>() {
458	                    Ok(slot) => {
459	                        let desc = *slot;
460	                        slot.release();
461	                        // SAFETY: the desc was consumed into
462	                        // the ring by into_desc above and is
463	                        // resolved exactly once, same thread.
464	                        let msg = unsafe { registry.resolve::<Msg>(desc) }.unwrap(); // OK: desc came from into_desc on this pool
465	                        assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
466	                        msg
467	                    }
468	                    Err(Empty) => {
469	                        panic!("spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_1t: consumer Empty SHOULD NOT HAPPEN");
470	                    }
471	                };
472	            }
473	            buf_slot.free();
474	        });
475	    });
476	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
477	}
478	
479	/// The composed flow producer-thread → consumer-thread:
480	/// alloc + fill pool messages on the producer, descriptors
481	/// cross the SPSC ring, the consumer resolves and frees;
482	/// return elapsed seconds.
483	///
484	/// - `pin` — `Some((p, c))` pins the producer to cpu `p` and
485	///   the consumer to cpu `c`; `None` lets the scheduler place
486	///   them (the number then depends on where they land).
487	fn spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_2t(pin: PinPair) -> f64 {
488	    let mut ring_region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
489	    let mut pool_region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
490	    let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut pool_region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH).unwrap(); // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the pool header + DEPTH buffers
491	    let mut registry = PoolRegistry::<1>::new();
492	    let pool_id = registry.register(pool.resolver()).unwrap(); // OK: empty capacity-1 registry always has room
493	    let registry = &registry;
494	    let (mut producer, mut consumer) =
495	        Ring::init(&mut ring_region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)
496	            .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots
497	            .split();
498	
499	    let start = Instant::now();
500	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
501	        s.spawn(move || {
502	            if let Some((p, _)) = pin {
503	                pin_to_cpu(p);
504	            }
505	            for i in 0..COUNT {
506	                let mut buf_slot = loop {
507	                    match pool.alloc::<Msg>() {
508	                        Ok(buf_slot) => break buf_slot,
509	                        Err(Exhausted) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
510	                    }
511	                };
512	                buf_slot.seq = i;
513	                let desc = registry
514	                    .into_desc(pool_id, buf_slot)
515	                    .map_err(|(_, e)| e)
516	                    .unwrap(); // OK: pool_id came from this registry's register
517	                loop {
518	                    match producer.reserve_slot::<Desc>() {
519	                        Ok(mut slot) => {
520	                            *slot = desc;
521	                            slot.commit();
522	                            break;
523	                        }
524	                        Err(Full) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
525	                    }
526	                }
527	            }
528	        });
529	        s.spawn(move || {
530	            if let Some((_, c)) = pin {
531	                pin_to_cpu(c);
532	            }
533	            for i in 0..COUNT {
534	                let desc = loop {
535	                    match consumer.reserve_slot::<Desc>() {
536	                        Ok(slot) => {
537	                            let desc = *slot;
538	                            slot.release();
539	                            break desc;
540	                        }
541	                        Err(Empty) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
542	                    }
543	                };
544	                // SAFETY: the desc was consumed into the ring
545	                // by the producer and read after the commit →
546	                // reserve handoff (happens-before); each is
547	                // resolved exactly once.
548	                let msg = unsafe { registry.resolve::<Msg>(desc) }.unwrap(); // OK: descs here only come from the producer's into_desc
549	                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
550	                msg.free();
551	            }
552	        });
553	    });
554	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
555	}
556	
557	/// Alloc + fill COUNT messages on an allocator thread,
558	/// free them on a freer thread (guards cross a std
559	/// channel); return elapsed seconds.
```

> AGENT

I've now got the full picture. Here are my thoughts before touching anything.

## Assessment: agree with the removal

The bug's own analysis is solid and I concur. `reserve_slot` is strictly dominated by `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)` (one probe, identical loads, folds away), and its real hazard is the *loop-on-fallible* usage pattern that costs the measured +30 ns / 2× jitter. Dropping it to a two-rung ladder (`reserve_slot_with` primitive + `reserve_slot_spin` convenience) is the right shape.

## What the removal actually touches

Only **one** non-test caller category and a pile of tests/docs:

- **Demo (`src/bin/zc-ring-x1-demo.rs`)** — 4 sites:
  - `*_1t` (L191/200, L448/457): single-probe, panic on failure → `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)` (semantically identical: one attempt).
  - `*_2t` (L235/252, L518/535): the exact loop-on-fallible + `spin_loop()` anti-pattern → collapses cleanly to `reserve_slot_spin()`.
- **Tests** — `lib.rs` (~15 sites) + `registry.rs` (2 sites): `.is_err()`/`.unwrap()` single-probes → `_with(|_| false)`; the loop-match pair → `reserve_slot_spin`.
- **Doc comments** — the hidden cost. Both `reserve_slot_with` and `reserve_slot_spin` docs currently define themselves *relative to* `reserve_slot` (`[reserve_slot](Consumer::reserve_slot)` intra-doc links), plus the `Empty`/`Consumer`/`ReadSlot` struct docs and `lib.rs:325`. Removing the method breaks those rustdoc links — `reserve_slot_with` has to be rewritten as the self-contained primitive.

Note the iiac-perf `zcr-raw-*` benches are a *separate* repo (tracked there per the bug's "Downstream"); this cycle only owns the in-repo demo + tests + docs.

## The one real judgment call

The bug says don't add a `try_reserve_slot` alias "before a real consumer needs it — no consumers exist yet." But the removal makes `_with(|_| false)` appear ~15+ times across tests/demo *immediately*. So:

1	//! Producing endpoint: [`Producer`] reserves the next free
2	//! slot, the [`WriteSlot`] guard writes it in place and
3	//! commits.
4	
5	use core::marker::PhantomData;
6	use core::ops::{Deref, DerefMut};
7	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
8	use zerocopy::{FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};
9	
10	use crate::{Header, USER_WORDS, check_type, slot_ptr};
11	
12	/// `reserve_slot` failed: every slot holds an
13	/// uncommitted-or-unread message.
14	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
15	pub struct Full;
16	
17	/// The producing endpoint: `reserve_slot`, write in place,
18	/// `commit`.
19	pub struct Producer<'a> {
20	    /// The ring's control block.
21	    header: &'a Header,
22	    /// Base of the slot array.
23	    slots: *mut u8,
24	    /// Geometry snapshot (see [`Ring`](crate::Ring)).
25	    slot_size: u32,
26	    /// Geometry snapshot (see [`Ring`](crate::Ring)).
27	    capacity: u32,
28	    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).
29	    mask: u32,
30	    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,
31	}
32	
33	// SAFETY: the handle owns the producer role; the shared state it
34	// touches (indices) is atomic, and slot writes are handed off
35	// with Release/Acquire ordering.
36	unsafe impl Send for Producer<'_> {}
37	
38	impl<'a> Producer<'a> {
39	    /// Build the handle from [`Ring::split`](crate::Ring::split)'s
40	    /// geometry snapshot.
41	    pub(crate) fn new(
42	        header: &'a Header,
43	        slots: *mut u8,
44	        slot_size: u32,
45	        capacity: u32,
46	        mask: u32,
47	    ) -> Self {
48	        Producer {
49	            header,
50	            slots,
51	            slot_size,
52	            capacity,
53	            mask,
54	            _region: PhantomData,
55	        }
56	    }
57	
58	    /// The header's app-owned scratch line: zeroed at init,
59	    /// never touched by the crate again.
60	    ///
61	    /// - Shared with the peer: treat contents as untrusted
62	    ///   data — store values, never addresses to dereference.
63	    /// - See the design doc's "Blocking and user words" for
64	    ///   the wakeup-protocol contract it exists to host.
65	    pub fn user(&self) -> &[AtomicU32; USER_WORDS] {
66	        &self.header.user
67	    }
68	
69	    /// Reserve the next free slot as a `&mut T`, or [`Full`].
70	    ///
71	    /// - Only one slot may be reserved at a time: the guard
72	    ///   holds the `&mut Producer` borrow, so a second
73	    ///   `reserve_slot` before the guard is dropped or
74	    ///   committed does not compile.
75	    /// - Dropping the guard without [`WriteSlot::commit`]
76	    ///   abandons the reservation (nothing published).
77	    pub fn reserve_slot<T>(&mut self) -> Result<WriteSlot<'_, T>, Full>
78	    where
79	        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,
80	    {
81	        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);
82	        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
83	        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);
84	        // `>=`, not `==`: a peer-corrupted consumer_idx makes
85	        // occupancy look huge — fail Full, never hand out a
86	        // slot the protocol doesn't own.
87	        if p.wrapping_sub(c) >= self.capacity {
88	            return Err(Full);
89	        }
90	        // Raw pointer, not `&mut T`: a reference field would be
91	        // argument-protected for the whole `commit(self)` call,
92	        // while commit's Release store lets the consumer read
93	        // the slot inside that window — UB (Miri-verified).
94	        // Deref mints short-lived references instead.
95	        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, p, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *mut T;
96	        Ok(WriteSlot {
97	            header: self.header,
98	            msg,
99	            next_idx: p.wrapping_add(1),
100	            _slot: PhantomData,
101	        })
102	    }
103	
104	    /// [`reserve_slot`](Producer::reserve_slot) with an
105	    /// injected wait policy: retry until a slot frees up or
106	    /// the policy gives up.
107	    ///
108	    /// - `on_full` is called after each failed attempt with
109	    ///   the attempt count (0-based, saturating); returning
110	    ///   `false` gives up → `Err(Full)`.
111	    /// - The wait loop is the reservation itself (the guard
112	    ///   borrows the endpoint, so retrying a `reserve_slot`
113	    ///   call could not return it): `producer_idx` is ours
114	    ///   and loaded once; only `consumer_idx` is re-read per
115	    ///   attempt, so the no-wait fast path does exactly the
116	    ///   loads `reserve_slot` does.
117	    /// - See [`policy`](crate::policy) for shipped policies
118	    ///   and the composition model.
119	    pub fn reserve_slot_with<T>(
120	        &mut self,
121	        mut on_full: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool,
122	    ) -> Result<WriteSlot<'_, T>, Full>
123	    where
124	        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,
125	    {
126	        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);
127	        // Ours alone: no peer moves it, load once.
128	        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
129	        let mut attempt = 0u32;
130	        loop {
131	            // Same load + `>=` occupancy check as
132	            // reserve_slot (see there for the corruption
133	            // rationale).
134	            let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);
135	            if p.wrapping_sub(c) < self.capacity {
136	                break;
137	            }
138	            if !on_full(attempt) {
139	                return Err(Full);
140	            }
141	            attempt = attempt.saturating_add(1);
142	        }
143	        // Raw pointer, not `&mut T` — see reserve_slot.
144	        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, p, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *mut T;
145	        Ok(WriteSlot {
146	            header: self.header,
147	            msg,
148	            next_idx: p.wrapping_add(1),
149	            _slot: PhantomData,
150	        })
151	    }
152	
153	    /// [`reserve_slot`](Producer::reserve_slot), spinning
154	    /// until a slot is available —
155	    /// [`reserve_slot_with`](Producer::reserve_slot_with)
156	    /// under the never-quitting
157	    /// [`policy::spin`](crate::policy::spin), so no `Result`.
158	    pub fn reserve_slot_spin<T>(&mut self) -> WriteSlot<'_, T>
159	    where
160	        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,
161	    {
162	        match self.reserve_slot_with(crate::policy::spin) {
163	            Ok(slot) => slot,
164	            Err(Full) => unreachable!("policy::spin never gives up"),
165	        }
166	    }
167	}
168	
169	/// A reserved write slot: `DerefMut` to write the message, then
170	/// [`commit`](WriteSlot::commit).
171	pub struct WriteSlot<'p, T> {
172	    /// The ring's control block (for the commit store).
173	    header: &'p Header,
174	    /// The slot, viewed as the message type. Raw on purpose —
175	    /// see the comment in [`Producer::reserve_slot`].
176	    msg: *mut T,
177	    /// Value `producer_idx` takes on commit.
178	    next_idx: u32,
179	    /// Owns the `&'p mut` borrow of the producer.
180	    _slot: PhantomData<&'p mut T>,
181	}
182	
183	impl<T> Deref for WriteSlot<'_, T> {
184	    type Target = T;
185	    /// Read access to the in-slot message.
186	    fn deref(&self) -> &T {
187	        // SAFETY: msg is in-bounds and aligned (check_type +
188	        // cache-line slot base), any byte pattern is a valid T
189	        // (FromBytes bound at reserve_slot), and the index
190	        // protocol gives this guard exclusive slot access until
191	        // commit.
192	        unsafe { &*self.msg }
193	    }
194	}
195	
196	impl<T> DerefMut for WriteSlot<'_, T> {
197	    /// Write access to the in-slot message.
198	    fn deref_mut(&mut self) -> &mut T {
199	        // SAFETY: as in deref; &mut self gives exclusivity of
200	        // the minted reference.
201	        unsafe { &mut *self.msg }
202	    }
203	}
204	
205	impl<T> WriteSlot<'_, T> {
206	    /// Publish the slot to the consumer
207	    /// (`producer_idx + 1`, `Release`).
208	    pub fn commit(self) {
209	        self.header
210	            .producer_idx
211	            .store(self.next_idx, Ordering::Release);
212	    }
213	}
214

554	    fn threaded_spsc() {
555	        // Reduced under Miri: interpreted spin loops are slow,
556	        // and its scheduler explores interleavings at any count.
557	        const COUNT: u64 = if cfg!(miri) { 200 } else { 100_000 };
558	        let mut r = Region::new();
559	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
560	
561	        std::thread::scope(|s| {
562	            s.spawn(move || {
563	                for i in 0..COUNT {
564	                    loop {
565	                        match prod.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
566	                            Ok(mut slot) => {
567	                                slot.seq = i;
568	                                slot.val = i * 3;
569	                                slot.commit();
570	                                break;
571	                            }
572	                            Err(Full) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
573	                        }
574	                    }
575	                }
576	            });
577	            s.spawn(move || {
578	                for i in 0..COUNT {
579	                    loop {
580	                        match cons.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
581	                            Ok(msg) => {
582	                                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
583	                                assert_eq!(msg.val, i * 3);
584	                                msg.release();
585	                                break;
586	                            }
587	                            Err(Empty) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
588	                        }
589	                    }
590	                }
591	            });
592	        });
593	    }
594	
595	    #[test]
596	    fn reserve_slot_with_policy_counts_and_gives_up() {
597	        let mut r = Region::new();
598	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
599	
600	        // Empty ring: the consumer's policy sees 0-based
601	        // attempts and its `false` becomes Err(Empty).
602	        let mut seen = Vec::new();
603	        let err = cons
604	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|attempt| {
605	                seen.push(attempt);
606	                attempt < 2
607	            })
608	            .err()
609	            .unwrap();
610	        assert_eq!(err, Empty);
611	        assert_eq!(seen, [0, 1, 2]);
612	
613	        // Fill the ring: the producer's policy gives up with
614	        // Err(Full).
615	        for i in 0..4 {
616	            let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
617	            slot.seq = i;
618	            slot.commit();
619	        }

1	# Versioning
2	
3	How this project versions its commits and the running
4	artifact. The scheme — the cycle step-numbering and the
5	unique-per-commit aim — is generic and shared across projects:
6	this file is copied **verbatim**, with
7	[Recording the version-of-record](#recording-the-version-of-record)
8	covering each medium by conditional rather than per-project
9	edits.
10	
11	## Terms
12	
13	Three names, used as defined here across
14	[AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md),
15	[cycle-protocol.md](cycle-protocol.md), and the notes files:
16	
17	- **version** — the per-commit version (e.g. `0.3.0-5.3.0`),
18	  written with `-` in prose, ladders, chores, todo, and commit
19	  talk; its suffix encodes the cycle phase (see
20	  [Step numbering](#step-numbering)).
21	- **version-of-record** — the authoritative stored copy of the
22	  version, in the project's manifest (see
23	  [Recording the version-of-record](#recording-the-version-of-record));
24	  a built or running artifact derives from it.
25	- **versioning** — the topic: this scheme as a whole.
26	
27	## Recording the version-of-record
28	
29	Where the version-of-record lives, how it's stored and
30	surfaced, and how often it changes — pick the case that fits
31	your medium:
32	
33	- **Manifest** — where the version-of-record is stored:
34	  - if Rust, `Cargo.toml` `[package].version`
35	  - if Python, `pyproject.toml` `[project].version` (or the
36	    committed config it's sourced from)
37	  - otherwise wherever the medium records it (a generic
38	    `version.toml`, a book's frontmatter, …) — add the case as
39	    needed
40	- **Notation** — how the `-` form is stored:
41	  - if the format allows `-` (TOML `version.toml`, `Cargo.toml`),
42	    store it verbatim
43	  - if it bars `-` (PEP 440's local segment, e.g. a Python
44	    project), remap to `+` — `0.3.0-5.3.0` → `0.3.0+5.3.0`: same
45	    version, just the stored spelling
46	- **Reporter** — how a built artifact surfaces the
47	  version-of-record:
48	  - if a CLI app, `<cli-app> -V`
49	  - if a TUI/GUI, add to Help/About or display on the title
50	- **Cadence** — how often to bump: see
51	  [Unique per commit](#unique-per-commit-preference-not-requirement);
52	  this project follows the per-commit preference.
53	
54	## Unique per commit (preference, not requirement)
55	
56	Our general notion is that the version-of-record should change
57	on **every commit**, so a built or running artifact identifies
58	the exact commit it came from.
59	
60	- This is a preference, **not** a hard requirement. A project
61	  following the cycle protocol may bump less often — once per
62	  cycle, only at release, and so on. Record the choice in
63	  [Recording the version-of-record](#recording-the-version-of-record)
64	  if it differs.
65	- It is achievable because the cycle's versions (below) are
66	  **pre-assignable**, unlike the git SHA, which a commit cannot
67	  contain (see the cycle protocol's
68	  [Commits backfill](cycle-protocol.md#commits-backfill)).
69	
70	## Step numbering
71	
72	The cycle (Preparation -> Work -> Close-out; see
73	[cycle-protocol.md](cycle-protocol.md)) encodes each commit's
74	phase in the version suffix — the **final identifier `0` marks
75	a Preparation**:
76	
77	- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation
78	- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits
79	- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)
80	
81	**Preparation is optional.** A lightweight cycle — no ladder,
82	no setup commit — skips `-0` and starts at `-1` (its first
83	Work commit). The same holds at every level: a sub-cycle
84	needing no Preparation omits its `.0` (see Nesting). One that
85	grows a Preparation later adds the `0` step without
86	renumbering siblings.
87	
88	Disambiguation:
89	
90	- `-10` — Work commit #10 (final identifier `10`), not a
91	  Preparation.
92	- `-1.0` — Preparation of the `-1` sub-cycle (final
93	  identifier `0`).
94	
95	**Nesting.** Sub-cycles append another level, recursively:
96	
97	- `X.Y.Z-3.0` — Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle
98	- `X.Y.Z-3.1`, `X.Y.Z-3.2` — its Work
99	- `X.Y.Z-3` — its Close-out
100	- `X.Y.Z-3.1.0` — Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle
101	
102	Bump the version-of-record at the start of each phase, so the
103	active phase is recorded — and, per the preference above,
104	every commit carries a distinct version.
105	
```

Here's where my head is, and the open questions I'd want your read on:

## The plan as I see it

**Core:** delete `reserve_slot` from both `Consumer` and `Producer`, leaving the two-rung ladder (`reserve_slot_with` primitive + `reserve_slot_spin` convenience) — exactly what bug #1's "Current thinking" prescribes.

**Call-site migration (all in-repo callers):**
- **Demo `*_1t`** (single-probe, panic-on-fail): `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)`
- **Demo `*_2t`** (loop + `spin_loop()`): collapses to `reserve_slot_spin()`
- **Tests** (`lib.rs`, `registry.rs`): `.is_err()`/`.unwrap()` single-probes → `_with(|_| false)`; loop-match → `reserve_slot_spin`

**Doc churn (the bit I want to flag):** `reserve_slot_with` and `reserve_slot_spin` currently *define themselves relative to* `reserve_slot` via `[reserve_slot](Consumer::reserve_slot)` intra-doc links. Removing the method breaks those rustdoc links, so `reserve_slot_with` has to be rewritten as the self-contained primitive. That's ~8 doc/comment touch-ups per endpoint file plus `lib.rs:325`. Mechanical, but it's the largest surface of the change.

## Things I actually want to discuss

1. **`threaded_spsc` test.** Right now it loops-on-fallible (the anti-pattern). There's *already* a `threaded_spsc_spin_variants` test covering the spin path. So do we (a) convert `threaded_spsc` to loop on `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)` — keeps a genuine "fallible primitive + external retry" contention test, complementary to the spin one — or (b) delete it as now-redundant with the spin variant? I lean (a).

2. **The `*_2t` demo benches.** Converting them to `reserve_slot_spin` changes what the *demo* measures (spin path, not raw-loop). The bug says the raw-loop measurement lives in iiac-perf's `zcr-raw-*`. Are you fine with the in-repo demo showcasing only the recommended API, or do you want to keep a raw-loop timing here too?

3. **Cycle scope.** This is code (fmt/clippy/test/install) — a full cycle starting from `0.7.3`. Is this the cycle you want to open now, or were you thinking something narrower?

1	# Cycle protocol
2	
3	This protocol uses [Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form). It
4	contains instructions on how a commit cycle is accomplished.
5	
6	The artifact a cycle produces is whatever the bot generates from
7	the conversation — code, prose, an image, a song, a screenplay.
8	The steps below use a Rust crate as the running example (the
9	cargo cycle, `Cargo.toml` versioning); substitute your medium's
10	equivalents — this project's manifest is recorded in
11	[versioning.md](versioning.md).
12	
13	## Cycles
14	
15	A cycle has three phases:
16	
17	- **[Preparation](#preparation)** (`X.Y.Z-0`) — the cycle's
18	  first commit, when it needs setup (a lightweight cycle omits
19	  it and starts at `-1` — see
20	  [versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering)). Sets up the
21	  cycle:
22	  - Bump the version-of-record to `X.Y.Z-0` (where it lives
23	    and the suffix scheme are project-specific — see
24	    [versioning.md](versioning.md)).
25	  - Pick up a `## Todo` item (typically the top-ranked,
26	    #1) into `## In Progress` (bold title + succinct problem
27	    statement + plan ladder).
28	  - Open the [chores section](#chores-sections).
29	- **[Work-N](#work-n)** (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …) — the
30	  commits that implement the change. As many as the change
31	  needs; each runs through the
32	  [per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow).
33	- **[Close-out](#close-out)** (bare `X.Y.Z`) — the cycle's
34	  last commit. Bookkeeping only:
35	  - Move the picked-up item to a one-line entry in
36	    `## Done`.
37	  - Move the `## In Progress` block into the
38	    [chores section](#chores-sections).
39	  - Optionally update `notes/README.md` if functionality
40	    changed.
41	
42	A cycle's commits are published to the project remote
43	either incrementally or as one batch at close-out; the
44	result must always be published at close-out. See
45	[Pushing](#pushing).
46	
47	**Sub-cycles.** When a Work commit's scope grows enough to
48	warrant its own ladder, it subdivides — `X.Y.Z-3.0`
49	Preparation, `X.Y.Z-3.1` / `X.Y.Z-3.2` Work, `X.Y.Z-3`
50	Close-out. The same three-phase shape applies recursively
51	at every depth. See [Numbering](#numbering) for the
52	suffix rule and [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)
53	for the local-ladder mechanics.
54	
55	## Chores sections
56	
57	A **chores section** is a `##` section in
58	`notes/chores/chores-NN.md` recording landed work. In
59	general, every commit that lands on the permanent branch
60	should have a reference to it on a `Commits:` list in a
61	chores file.
62	
63	The phrase **"Open" the chores section** means append a
64	`##` header to the current `notes/chores/chores-NN.md`
65	with the title it records (e.g. `## refactor: foo bar`),
66	followed by an **empty `Commits:`** line. The narrative
67	body stays empty until close-out (see [Close-out](#close-out));
68	the `Commits:` line is backfilled later, once the commit is
69	permanent (see [Commits backfill](#commits-backfill) below).
70	
71	Fuller chores conventions (content rules, header sync,
72	design subsection pattern, `Commits:` formatting) live in
73	AGENTS.md [Chores conventions](../AGENTS.md#chores-conventions).
74	
75	### Commits backfill
76	
77	A chores section's `Commits:` line cites the commit(s) it
78	records, by SHA — but a SHA isn't stable until the commit
79	lands on a **permanent branch** (`main`, or a long-lived
80	release/patch branch that won't be rewritten); a rebase or
81	squash rewrites it on the way. So:
82	
83	- A chores section is **opened with an empty `Commits:`** line.
84	- **Backfill once the commit is on a permanent branch**,
85	  where its SHA is final. A commit can't record its own SHA
86	  (that would change the hash), so the fill always lands one
87	  push later: **each push backfills the `Commits:` of the
88	  commits the previous push made permanent.** On a topic
89	  branch the sections instead wait until the branch lands —
90	  so no SHA is ever written that a later rebase could
91	  invalidate.
92	
93	Use `[[N]]` refs — several as `[[N]],[[M]]` only when one
94	section records multiple commits (a merge non-ff close-out) —
95	with the commit URL + 40-hex SHA in the file's `# References`
96	(format in AGENTS.md
97	[Chores commit references](../AGENTS.md#chores-commit-references)).
98	A section's `##` title matches its commit title, so a rare
99	deliberate rewrite of a permanent-branch commit re-syncs via
100	`git log --grep "<title>"`.
101	
102	The per-push cadence is a project choice, not dogma — a
103	**per-close-out** model (recording a cycle's SHAs at its
104	close-out) is equally valid. The one invariant: a recorded
105	SHA must be permanent.
106	
107	## Preparation
108	
109	The cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`), when the cycle needs
110	setup (a lightweight cycle omits it — see
111	[versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering)):
112	
113	- **Bump the version-of-record** to `X.Y.Z-0`. Where it
114	  lives, the suffix scheme, and any derived files (a
115	  lockfile, a sourced manifest version) are
116	  project-specific — see [versioning.md](versioning.md).
117	- **Move a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one) into
118	  `## In Progress` and the todo item should have:
119	  - A **bold title line** — that will be the chores
120	    section header, minus the `## ` prefix.
121	  - A **succinct problem statement**; add if one is needed
122	  - A **plan ladder**.
123	- **Open the [chores section](#chores-sections)** —
124	  append a `##` header with the title it records — the
125	  cycle's anticipated close-out title.
126	
127	## Work-N
128	
129	The cycle's work commits (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …)
130	implement the change. As many as needed:
131	
132	- Each commit runs through the
133	  **[per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow)**.
134	- **Interim pushes** are optional (backup, progress
135	  visibility).
136	- Close-out is the only mandatory push (see
137	  [Pushing](#pushing)).
138	- **Subdivide into a sub-cycle** if a Work commit's
139	  scope grows enough (see
140	  [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)).
141	
142	## Close-out
143	
144	The cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`) does bookkeeping
145	only — the commit body describes that bookkeeping, not
146	what happens post-squash:
147	
148	- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a
149	  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.
150	- **Move the `## In Progress` block into the
151	  [chores section](#chores-sections)** (the title-only
152	  placeholder opened during Preparation):
153	  - Cut the problem statement + plan ladder out of
154	    `## In Progress` (drop the bold title line — the
155	    chores `##` header already carries that string).
156	  - Paste under the chores `##` header.
157	  - Sync the chores header to the **final** commit title
158	    if the cycle's scope shifted; update every anchor
159	    back-reference.
160	  - Add an `### As-built ladder` listing the cycle's
161	    commits; optional `### Outcome` notes.
162	  - Replace `## In Progress` with
163	    `_No cycle currently in progress._`.
164	- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed
165	  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).
166	
167	Whether to **squash** the cycle into one commit before the
168	publishing push, or push as-is, is decided at push time —
169	see [Pushing](#pushing).
170	
171	## Numbering
172	
173	Each commit's phase is encoded in the version suffix — `-0`
174	Preparation, `-1`/`-2`/… Work, bare `X.Y.Z` Close-out,
175	recursively for sub-cycles. The full scheme — disambiguation,
176	nesting, optional Preparation, the project's version-of-record
177	format, and the per-phase bump — lives in
178	[versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering), which is the
179	single source of truth for this repo's versioning.
180	
181	## Per-commit flow
182	
183	Every commit (Preparation, each Work commit, Close-out) goes
184	through:
185	
186	1. **Mark this commit `(current)`** as the first edit in
187	   `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`.
188	2. **Do the work** (see [Iterative work](#iterative-work)
189	   for the loop-and-squash technique).
190	3. **Flip this commit `(current)` → `(done)`** in `## In
191	   Progress` — before the cargo cycle and the commit.
192	4. **Validate the artifact** — a medium-specific step, skip-able
193	   for notes-only commits, mandatory at close-out. For the Rust
194	   example the cargo cycle is:
195	   1. `cargo fmt`
196	   2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`
197	   3. `cargo test`
198	   4. `cargo install --path . --locked`
199	   5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)
200	5. **Work review.** Stop *before* writing any description;
201	   tell the user "ready to commit." The user reviews the
202	   changes and we iterate until complete.
203	6. **Write the commit description** — see
204	   [Commit description](#commit-description).
205	7. **Commit Description review.** Show the title + body
206	   and stop. The user reviews the description. Iterate.
207	8. **Commit.** `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .` for
208	   the app repo, `-R .claude` for the bot repo (`-R` last
209	   keeps the verb visible):
210	
211	   ```
212	   jj commit -m \
213	   "<type>: <short description>" \
214	   -m "<intro paragraph>
215	
216	   - file1: gist
217	   - file2: gist
218	
219	   ochid: /.claude/<chid>" \
220	   -R .
221	   ```
222	
223	**Two overrides apply:**
224	
225	- **Deviation or question** — any time the work deviates
226	  from the agreed plan, or a question arises, stop and
227	  surface it; don't push through.
228	- **ESC-ESC** — the user can interrupt at any point to pull
229	  a review or question forward.
230	
231	## Commit description
232	
233	[Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/):
234	
235	```
236	<type>: <short description>
237	```
238	
239	Titles carry **no trailing `(<version>)` suffix**. The
240	version-of-record — where it lives and its bump cadence, see
241	[versioning.md](versioning.md) — is useful for confirming
242	you're running the version you're testing, not a per-commit
243	title marker. We think per-commit version-in-title is
244	unstable on large projects: many changes are in flight
245	simultaneously, and a
246	version is only stable once merged into the main repo — so
247	titles don't carry one.
248	
249	### Title
250	
251	- ≤50 chars total.
252	- Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `test`,
253	  `docs`, `chore`.
254	- Favor terse phrasings.
255	- **Distinct per step** — each of a cycle's commits gets its
256	  own descriptive title (no shared cycle title with a step
257	  marker). Share a greppable stem across the cycle's titles
258	  (e.g. `ring buffer`) so `git log --grep` collects them; the
259	  chores section header matches the close-out title. See
260	  AGENTS.md
261	  [Conventional-commit shape](../AGENTS.md#conventional-commit-shape-ladder--chores--commit).
262	
263	### Body
264	
265	[Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form) (intro + bullets),
266	wrap ≤72. Bullet content differs per repo:
267	
268	- **App-repo body**: file-by-file. One bullet per file
269	  changed (file plus a one-line gist). Sub-bullets for
270	  files with multiple distinct changes:
271	
272	  ```
273	  - path/to/file1
274	    - first distinct change
275	    - second distinct change, wrapping to next line
276	       with continuation indented 5
277	  ```
278	
279	  The file-by-file list is the source of truth for the
280	  cycle's mechanical change record. Chores carries the
281	  narrative + design, not a copy of it. Promote any
282	  "why" beyond one sentence to a chores `###` subsection.
283	
284	- **Session-repo (`.claude`) body**: bullets describe
285	  in-session activity rather than code changes.
286	
287	### Trailer
288	
289	`ochid:` as the last line of the body — see
290	[Cross-repo linking (ochid trailers)](../AGENTS.md#cross-repo-linking-ochid-trailers)
291	in AGENTS.md for the convention.
292	
293	For breaking changes, use the hyphenated `BREAKING-CHANGE:`
294	trailer key. `BREAKING CHANGE:` (with a space) is the only
295	space-separated key the Conventional Commits spec allows; the
296	hyphenated form is also valid and avoids the space ambiguity.
297	
298	## Reviewing changes
299	
300	Work review looks at the **uncommitted working-copy diff**,
301	on the way to commit. The user opens diffs in their
302	editor (Zed, VSCode); jj commands are for terminal:
303	
304	- `jj diff` — working-copy diff (uncommitted)
305	- `jj diff -r @-` — diff of the previous commit
306	- `jj diff --from <X> --to <Y>` — any two revisions
307	- `jj show -r <X>` — description + diff for one rev
308	
309	Don't `jj edit -r @-` to view a past commit — that marks
310	it mutable and shifts `@`; use `jj diff -r @-` or
311	`jj show -r @-`.
312	
313	See [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders) for the
314	close-out squash recipe and recovery; revset primitives
315	are in [`jj-tips.md`](jj-tips.md#revsets).
316	
317	## Pushing
318	
319	### Policy
320	
321	Push is **discretionary** during the cycle (backup,
322	progress visibility) and **mandatory at close-out** —
323	the cycle's result must be published.
324	
325	**Approval is per-push.** Every push — any repo, any kind:
326	cycle push, interim backup, recovery/surgery force-push —
327	happens only after the user has reviewed the changes to be
328	published and explicitly approved that specific push.
329	Approval of a plan that *includes* a push does not authorize
330	the push itself; stop and ask again at the moment of pushing.
331	
332	**Default is interactive; an explicit scoped delegation waives
333	the gates.** The gates above — per-push approval, the
334	commit-description review (show title+body and stop), and the
335	hard stop after push/finalize — are the *interactive default*.
336	They yield when the user **explicitly** delegates a complete,
337	bounded task and authorizes carrying it through ("do all of X
338	and push each step, don't check in"). The bot then proceeds
339	through that task's commits and pushes without stopping, and
340	continues past each push to the next step. Conditions:
341	
342	- **Explicit grant** — never inferred from a task merely being
343	  well-scoped; the user's words must authorize unattended
344	  completion. "Commit and push" (or "then push") names the
345	  destination, not a waiver: it authorizes the push *after*
346	  the normal work review and description review, not skipping
347	  them. Only wording that explicitly waives the stops ("don't
348	  check in", "no need to review", "carry it through
349	  unattended") waives them.
350	- **Bounded goal** — covers the named task only; does not carry
351	  to the next task or a vaguer follow-on.
352	- **Destructive ops still pause** — delegation covers the task's
353	  ordinary commits and pushes; it does *not* pre-authorize a
354	  genuinely irreversible action (force-push over published
355	  history, history rewrite, deleting a remote branch). Those can
356	  permanently destroy work and aren't a normal cycle step, so the
357	  bot flags one before acting. An ordinary delegated cycle never
358	  reaches this.
359	- **Still transparent** — report each commit/push as it lands
360	  (title + outcome) so the user can catch up.
361	- **When in doubt, ask** — ambiguous authorization falls back to
362	  per-push approval.
363	
364	### Shape at close-out push
365	
366	At close-out the cycle's *work* is done; its *published
367	shape* is the remaining choice, made at push time. Surface
368	the options and get user approval before pushing — once on
369	the target, changing shape is a remote rewrite (force-push,
370	needs approval), so choose deliberately.
371	
372	- **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target.
373	  Right for straightforward changes where the Work-N is
374	  focused on one or two files.
375	- **Merge non-ff** *(current default)* — `main` gains a
376	  merge commit (`X.Y.Z`); cycle commits stay reachable via
377	  two parents. `jj log -r ..@ -n <N>` shows the trapezoidal
378	  shape. See [Merge non-ff recipe](#merge-non-ff-recipe)
379	  for the full setup sequence.
380	- **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry on
381	  `main`. Use when the decomposition itself is
382	  informative. Each chores section keeps its own header /
383	  `Commits:` ref; no consolidation churn.
384	
385	Set up squash/merge before invoking `vc-x1 push`; use
386	`jj git push` directly for non-standard shapes.
387	
388	### Merge non-ff recipe
389	
390	Setting up a [Merge non-ff](#shape-at-close-out-push)
391	close-out is a fixed sequence. `<closeout>` is the cycle's
392	close-out commit, `<prev>` the previous cycle's close-out
393	(the current `main` tip), `<work-tip>` the cycle's last
394	Work commit:
395	
396	1. **Rebase the close-out into a merge** — `jj rebase -r
397	   <closeout> --onto <prev> --onto <work-tip>`.
398	   - `-r <closeout>` keeps the `<closeout>` commit in place.
399	   - `--onto <prev>` becomes its first parent (trunk).
400	   - `--onto <work-tip>` becomes the second parent.
401	
402	   Together these make `<closeout>` a merge of
403	   `<prev>` + `<work-tip>`, forming a trapezoidal commit.
404	2. **Use `jj new <merge>`** to add an empty `@` above the
405	   merge. The rebase left `@` *on* the now-content-bearing
406	   merge, which git/IDE diff views show as uncommitted;
407	   `jj new` restores the clean empty `@` on top.
408	3. **Push** — `jj git push --bookmark main -R .`.
409	
410	**Post-hoc caveat.** If the cycle was already pushed
411	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) its commits are
412	immutable: the rebase needs `--ignore-immutable` and the
413	push force-updates `main`.
414	The standard sequence assumes the merge is set up *before*
415	the close-out push.
416	
417	### vc-x1 push wrapper
418	
419	`vc-x1 push <bookmark>` wraps per-push mechanics. See
420	`vc-x1 push --help` for current flags.
421	
422	**Current limitation**: only fully supports the
423	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) shape; other
424	shapes need manual jj steps. Improvements tracked /
425	planned:
426	
427	- N:1 code↔bot for Merge non-ff (`## Todo` entry P1).
428	- Symmetric squash (planned, to be captured in `-2`).
429	- Per-repo bookmark names (planned).
430	
431	### .claude cadence
432	
433	**Cadence**: one push = one `.claude` commit, paired
434	with every code commit in that push.
435	
436	The `.claude` working copy accumulates session data
437	across the cycle; its change ID stays stable across
438	snapshots, `jj describe`, and the finalize commit, so
439	code-side `ochid:` trailers resolve.
440	
441	`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives
442	on `main`, regardless of the app-side bookmark. **Do not
443	create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror
444	app-side branches** — risks the bot steering session
445	pushes to the wrong remote ref.
446	
447	Ending a session: if the user runs `/exit` there will be
448	session information created, which we don't worry about.
449	The user can close the terminal instead and `@` will
450	remain empty.
451	
452	### Bot communication at the reviews
453	
454	Use plain prose — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal",
455	"Checkpoint N", etc.):
456	
457	- **At Work review** — summarize what changed and stop.
458	  "Work complete. Please review."
459	- **At Commit Description review** — present `$TITLE`
460	  and `$BODY` explicitly; ask permission to commit/push.
461	  Don't spell out the full `vc-x1 push ... --title ...
462	  --body ...` invocation by default.
463	- **At Post close-out review** — surface the shape
464	  options (squash / merge / keep) and the push target;
465	  wait for the user's choice before any `jj squash` /
466	  `jj rebase` / `jj git push` invocation.
467	
468	### After push or finalize: stop and wait
469	
470	After you **push** (cross the remote boundary) or launch
471	**finalize** on the `.claude` repo (`vc-x1 finalize --repo
472	.claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach`) — by
473	hand or via the `vc-x1 push` wrapper — you **MUST NEVER**
474	proceed (next step, edit, tool call, text output) until the
475	user explicitly directs you to continue. **Even when the next
476	step seems obvious — wait.** Treat push-or-finalize as a hard
477	stop for the whole turn.
478	
479	`vc-x1 push` performs both as its tail stages — `push-app`
480	then the detached `vc-x1 finalize` on `.claude` — so there is
481	no gap left to speak in once the push is invoked. Put **all**
482	closing words *before* invoking it. The harness rejects an empty
483	turn, so it may force a visible token after the tool returns; if
484	so, emit a bare acknowledgment only (e.g. "landed") — never a
485	summary, verification, or next-step offer.
486	
487	Why (`.claude` is the live journal finalize snapshots →
488	squashes → pushes):
489	
490	- **`--detach`**: lets the tool return immediately so the
491	  harness can keep flushing the *trailing* session-data — the
492	  tool call's own transcript and any words emitted *before*
493	  the invoke — into the snapshot.
494	- **`--delay 10`**: gives the harness time to complete that
495	  flush before the snapshot is taken. It is **not** time for
496	  the bot to do more work.
497	- **silence**: the bot must not summarize or perform any work
498	  after the tool returns. The harness may force a visible token
499	  (it rejects an empty turn) — keep it to a bare acknowledgment,
500	  never a summary or a next step.
501	
502	**Known slip**: the bot has emitted a summary after launching
503	finalize. There is no "harmless" closing line — if it's worth
504	saying, say it *before* the invoke.
505	
506	### Recovery
507	
508	- **If push exits before `finalize-claude`** (e.g.
509	  failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run
510	  finalize by hand:
511	
512	  ```
513	  vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
514	  ```
515	
516	- **Run finalize again if `@` is non-empty** after the
517	  finalize's squash-and-commit (also desirable after
518	  extra activity by the bot's agents).
519	  - Why: finalize snapshots `.claude` at `--delay`, but the
520	    bot keeps writing session data while it runs — the tool's
521	    own invocation plus the bot's closing response land
522	    *after* the snapshot.
523	  - Safe to repeat: bot session data is append-only, so a
524	    re-run never conflicts or overwrites. (This could
525	    change; it is not under the user's control.)
526	  - Fix: run `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push
527	    <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach` another time to capture
528	    that tail; one extra pass is generally enough.
529	  - No guarantees: events outside the bot's control can leave
530	    `@` non-empty — e.g. the bot's back end may decide to
531	    squash/consolidate session data, which can take minutes
532	    and land after the snapshot. The remedy is the same: just
533	    run finalize again. This is why a single pass is never
534	    guaranteed to leave `@` empty.
535	- **Clear push's saved state** after any out-of-band
536	  recovery — `rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml` or `vc-x1 push
537	  <bookmark> --restart` — otherwise push resumes from a
538	  stale stage.
539	- **Late code-repo tweak after `push-app` succeeded**
540	  (e.g. updating AGENTS.md or memory) requires `jj
541	  squash --ignore-immutable` and a re-push; that is a
542	  remote rewrite and needs explicit approval like any
543	  push.
544	
545	## Iterative work
546	
547	When work for a single commit (the **target**) benefits
548	from incremental review, loop:
549	
550	1. `jj new -R .` — fresh empty `@` on top of the target.
551	2. Make the next round of changes.
552	3. User reviews the round (see
553	   [Reviewing changes](#reviewing-changes)).
554	4. `jj squash -R .` folds into the target and creates a
555	   new empty `@`.
556	5. If not done, go to step 2.
557	
558	Same jj mechanics as a
559	[sub-cycle ladder](#sub-cycle-ladders), but at
560	single-commit scope — the version
561	doesn't change.
562	
563	## Sub-cycle ladders
564	
565	When a Work commit subdivides into a sub-cycle (see
566	[Numbering](#numbering) for suffix nesting), its Work
567	commits typically live as a local jj `@` chain and
568	**collapse into the sub-cycle's Close-out** before the
569	parent cycle continues. Ladder commits are scratch —
570	for review and bisection only.
571	
572	### Per-Work-commit contract within a ladder
573	
574	For each Work commit in the ladder:
575	
576	1. `jj new -R .` — create a fresh empty `@`.
577	2. Do the commit's work.
578	3. Run the fast validation (Rust example: `cargo test
579	   --bins`). **Non-negotiable** — for code, build and clippy
580	   alone miss regressions until a later commit runs the full
581	   suite, raising bisection cost.
582	4. `jj describe -m "..." -m "..." -R .` — working title
583	   only; the sub-cycle Close-out collects everything
584	   into one final commit.
585	
586	### Navigating the ladder
587	
588	Common moves:
589	
590	- `jj log -r '<base>::' -R .` — see the whole ladder
591	  from its base.
592	- `jj edit -r <prefix> -R .` — jump `@` to any ladder
593	  commit by chid prefix; useful for bisection.
594	- `jj edit @-- -R .` — quick-jump back two commits.
595	- `jj diff -r <chid> -R .` — review one commit in
596	  isolation.
597	
598	Modifications to any ladder commit rewrite it in place;
599	descendants auto-rebase.
600	
601	### Close-out: squash the ladder
602	
603	When all ladder Work commits are done and tests pass:
604	
605	```
606	jj squash --from "<base>..@-" --into @ -u -R .
607	```
608	
609	`<base>` is the parent of the first ladder commit; `-u`
610	keeps `@`'s description and discards the sources'.
611	After squash, history is linear: `<base> → @`;
612	intermediate commits are auto-abandoned.
613	
614	Then `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` as for any other commit.
615	
616	For N = 1 the squash is a no-op (`<base>..@-` is empty
617	when `@-` is `<base>`); push the single commit directly.
618	
619	### Recovery
620	
621	If a ladder commit goes wrong, back out without losing
622	prior commits:
623	
624	- **Discard the current commit.** `jj abandon @ -R .`
625	  drops it; you get a fresh empty `@` on the same
626	  parent.
627	- **Edit an earlier commit.** `jj edit -r <chid> -R .`,
628	  make corrections, then `jj edit -r <last-ladder-chid>`
629	  to return. Descendants auto-rebase.
630	- **Discard the entire ladder.** `jj op log -R .` shows
631	  the op history; `jj op restore <op-id> -R .` reverts
632	  to that point. Full undo — removes *all* ladder work
633	  after the chosen op. Use only to start over.
634	
635	# References
636	
637	- [`jj-tips.md`](jj-tips.md#revsets) — revset primitives
638	  (chid/cid, `@`/`@-`/`@+`, `..`/`::` ranges, prefix matching).
639	- [`substep-test.sh`](substep-test.sh) — script that
640	  scaffolds a 4-revision ladder under `/tmp/substep-test`
641	  for squash-recipe experiments.
642	- The per-commit `cargo test --bins` gate exists because a
643	  regression introduced in an early ladder commit can go
644	  uncaught until a later commit runs the full suite, raising
645	  bisection cost.
646	
```

**Picked-up item:** bug #1 in `notes/bugs.md` (not a `## Todo` entry — so close-out removes it from `bugs.md` rather than moving a Todo to Done).

**Key structural constraint:** the core removal and the caller migration **must be one atomic Work commit** — deleting `reserve_slot` while any test/demo still calls it breaks `cargo test`, and the per-commit flow requires the suite to pass. So this is a 3-phase cycle with a single Work commit, not a multi-step ladder.

### Phases

- **`0.8.0-0` Preparation** (notes-only, skips cargo cycle)
  - Bump `Cargo.toml` → `0.8.0-0`
  - Open `## In Progress` block for bug #1 (problem statement + one-step ladder)
  - Open chores section in `chores-01.md` with empty `Commits:`

- **`0.8.0-1` Work** — the atomic refactor (full cargo cycle: fmt → clippy → test → install)
  - `consumer.rs` / `producer.rs`: delete `reserve_slot`; rewrite `reserve_slot_with` as the self-contained primitive (absorb the raw-pointer rationale + a `|_| false` single-probe note); repoint the now-dangling intra-doc links in `reserve_slot_spin`, the `Empty`/`Full` docstrings, the struct docstrings, and `ReadSlot.msg`/`WriteSlot.msg`

  - `registry.rs`: test loop-match → `reserve_slot_spin`
  - demo `*_1t` → `_with(|_| false)`; demo `*_2t` → `reserve_slot_spin`

- **`0.8.0` Close-out** (bookkeeping)
  - Remove bug #1 from `bugs.md` (+ renumber); `## Done` entry; move In Progress → chores; add `### As-built ladder`

  "old_string": "    /// Reserve the oldest unread slot as a `&T`, or [`Empty`].\n    ///\n    /// - Only one slot may be reserved at a time: the guard\n    ///   holds the `&mut Consumer` borrow, so a second\n    ///   `reserve_slot` before the guard is dropped or released\n    ///   does not compile.\n    /// - Dropping the guard without [`ReadSlot::release`]\n    ///   leaves the slot unread — the next `reserve_slot`\n    ///   returns it again.\n    pub fn reserve_slot<T>(&mut self) -> Result<ReadSlot<'_, T>, Empty>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable,\n    {\n        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);\n        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);\n        if p == c {\n            return Err(Empty);\n        }\n        // Raw pointer, not `&T`, for the same reason as in the\n        // producer's reserve_slot: release's store frees the\n        // slot for producer writes while a reference field\n        // would still be argument-protected.\n        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, c, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *const T;\n        Ok(ReadSlot {\n            header: self.header,\n            msg,\n            next_idx: c.wrapping_add(1),\n            _slot: PhantomData,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// [`reserve_slot`](Consumer::reserve_slot) with an\n    /// injected wait policy: retry until a message arrives or\n    /// the policy gives up.\n    ///\n    /// - `on_empty` is called after each failed attempt with\n    ///   the attempt count (0-based, saturating); returning\n    ///   `false` gives up → `Err(Empty)`.\n    /// - The wait loop is the reservation itself (the guard\n    ///   borrows the endpoint, so retrying a `reserve_slot`\n    ///   call could not return it): `consumer_idx` is ours\n    ///   and loaded once; only `producer_idx` is re-read per\n    ///   attempt, so the no-wait fast path does exactly the\n    ///   loads `reserve_slot` does.\n    /// - See [`policy`](crate::policy) for shipped policies\n    ///   and the composition model.\n    pub fn reserve_slot_with<T>(\n        &mut self,\n        mut on_empty: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool,\n    ) -> Result<ReadSlot<'_, T>, Empty>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable,\n    {\n        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);\n        // Ours alone: no peer moves it, load once.\n        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let mut attempt = 0u32;\n        loop {\n            let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);\n            if p != c {\n                break;\n            }\n            if !on_empty(attempt) {\n                return Err(Empty);\n            }\n            attempt = attempt.saturating_add(1);\n        }\n        // Raw pointer, not `&T` — see reserve_slot.\n        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, c, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *const T;",
  "new_string": "    /// Reserve the oldest unread slot as a `&T`, applying an\n    /// injected wait policy: retry until a message arrives or\n    /// the policy gives up → [`Empty`].\n    ///\n    /// - Only one slot may be reserved at a time: the guard\n    ///   holds the `&mut Consumer` borrow, so a second\n    ///   reservation before the guard is dropped or released\n    ///   does not compile.\n    /// - Dropping the guard without [`ReadSlot::release`]\n    ///   leaves the slot unread — the next reservation\n    ///   returns it again.\n    /// - `on_empty` is called after each failed attempt with\n    ///   the attempt count (0-based, saturating); returning\n    ///   `false` gives up → `Err(Empty)`. Pass `|_| false`\n    ///   for a single non-blocking probe.\n    /// - The wait loop is the reservation itself (the guard\n    ///   borrows the endpoint, so retrying could not return\n    ///   it): `consumer_idx` is ours and loaded once; only\n    ///   `producer_idx` is re-read per attempt.\n    /// - See [`policy`](crate::policy) for shipped policies\n    ///   and the composition model.\n    pub fn reserve_slot_with<T>(\n        &mut self,\n        mut on_empty: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool,\n    ) -> Result<ReadSlot<'_, T>, Empty>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable,\n    {\n        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);\n        // Ours alone: no peer moves it, load once.\n        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let mut attempt = 0u32;\n        loop {\n            let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);\n            if p != c {\n                break;\n            }\n            if !on_empty(attempt) {\n                return Err(Empty);\n            }\n            attempt = attempt.saturating_add(1);\n        }\n        // Raw pointer, not `&T`: release's store frees the slot\n        // for producer writes while a reference field would\n        // still be argument-protected. Deref mints short-lived\n        // references instead.\n        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, c, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *const T;"
}

1	//! Demo binary: both primitives working across threads,
2	//! with throughput printed — run `cargo run --release`, or
3	//! `cargo install --path . --locked` and run
4	//! `zc-ring-x1-demo`. `-V`/`--version` prints the
5	//! version-of-record so you know exactly which build you
6	//! are testing.
7	//!
8	//! - Part 1, the ring: an SPSC pair moves typed messages
9	//!   in place (reserve_slot → write → commit; reserve_slot
10	//!   → read → release) — first both ends on one thread
11	//!   (the ring's own cost), then one producer thread to
12	//!   one consumer thread: unpinned, pinned to two different
13	//!   physical cores, and pinned to one physical core's two
14	//!   SMT siblings (shared L1/L2, when the CPU has SMT).
15	//! - Part 2, the pool: an allocator thread allocs and
```

1	//! Throwaway compile/run check of the README example.
2	
3	use zc_ring_x1::Ring;
4	use zerocopy::{FromBytes, Immutable, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};
5	
6	#[derive(FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout, Immutable)]
7	#[repr(C)]
8	struct Msg {
9	    seq: u64,
10	    val: u64,
11	}
12	
13	/// Runs the README snippet.
14	fn main() {
15	    // Cache-line-aligned region: 256 B header + 4 slots × 64 B.
16	    #[repr(C, align(64))]
17	    struct Region([u8; 512]);
18	    let mut region = Region([0; 512]);
19	
20	    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
21	
22	    let mut slot = producer.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
23	    slot.seq = 1;
24	    slot.val = 42;
25	    slot.commit(); // publish to the consumer
26	
27	    let msg = consumer.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
28	    assert_eq!(msg.val, 42);
29	    msg.release(); // slot is free for reuse
30	    println!("readme example ok");
31	}
32

40	  the layout should not preclude adding it later.
41	- **In-place access API** — `reserve_slot` on either endpoint,
42	  then commit (producer) or release (consumer), exposing
43	  references into the buffer rather than returning values by
44	  move.
45	- **Safety** — all `unsafe` encapsulated inside the crate with
46	  documented invariants. The message path (reserve_slot, then
47	  commit / release) is fully safe; the one `unsafe` public
48	  entry
49	  is `Ring::attach` — see [API](#api) for why it cannot be

id: toolu_01MbEFF9BfiwrAq366inXiAN
```
180	  of two:
181	  - `2^32 % M == 0`, so occupancy math and masked slot
182	    positions both carry across the wrap unchanged
183	- **Example** (M = 4, `p` = producer_idx, `c` = consumer_idx):
184	  - start: `p = 0, c = 0` — occupancy 0, empty
185	  - producer commits 4 messages: `p = 4, c = 0` — occupancy
186	    4 == M, full; slot positions written were 0, 1, 2, 3
187	  - consumer releases 1: `p = 4, c = 1` — occupancy 3, one
188	    slot free; the next reserve_slot writes slot `4 & 3 == 0`
189	
190	Ordering: the producer loads `consumer_idx` with `Acquire`
191	and publishes `producer_idx` with `Release`; the consumer
192	mirrors (loads `producer_idx` `Acquire`, publishes
193	`consumer_idx` `Release`).
194	
195	## API
196	
197	Construction (resolves the foreign-memory question):
198	
199	- `Ring::init(region: &'a mut [u8], slot_size: u32, capacity:
200	  u32) -> Result<Ring<'a>, Error>` — validates geometry
201	  (power-of-two M, line-multiple N, alignment, region big
202	  enough — sized in u64, since the N×M product can wrap a
203	  32-bit usize), then writes the header per the
204	  [handshake](#memory-layout) (indices zeroed, magic last).
205	- `unsafe Ring::attach(region: *mut u8, len: usize) ->
206	  Result<Ring<'a>, Error>` — validates magic (Acquire),
207	  layout_version, and geometry against the region length,
208	  over the same shared `header_ptr` checks as init. Unsafe,
209	  deliberately, on two grounds:
210	  - zerocopy's shared validated casts require `Immutable`,
211	    which `Header` correctly is not (atomics) — so the cast
212	    is hand-verified rather than derive-verified;
213	  - only the caller can vouch that the pointer is a live,
214	    genuinely shared, writable mapping (e.g. `MAP_SHARED`)
215	    and that the SPSC role contract holds across processes.
216	- `ring.split() -> (Producer<'_>, Consumer<'_>)` — consumes
217	  the `Ring`, so each handle exists at most once per ring per
218	  process (SPSC discipline; cross-process there is one
219	  producer process and one consumer process by contract).
220	- **Geometry snapshot** — slot_size / capacity / mask are
221	  copied into `Ring` and the handles at init/attach; per-op
222	  paths never re-read peer-writable header fields. Combined
223	  with the all-atomic header, a hostile peer can degrade the
224	  ring to garbage messages or spurious Full/Empty — never UB.
225	
226	Producer — reserve_slot/commit, in place:
227	
228	- `producer.reserve_slot::<T>() ->
229	  Result<WriteSlot<'_, T>, Full>` — next free slot as `&mut T`
230	  (zerocopy `FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout`); `T`
231	  geometry is asserted against the slot size on each call (a
232	  mismatch is a programming error, so it panics; typed
233	  endpoints that check once are a follow-on — see
234	  [todo.md](todo.md)).
235	- `WriteSlot::commit(self)` — publishes the slot
236	  (`producer_idx + 1`, `Release`). Dropping without commit
237	  abandons the reservation (nothing published).
238	
239	Consumer — reserve_slot/release, in place:
240	
241	- `consumer.reserve_slot::<T>() ->
242	  Result<ReadSlot<'_, T>, Empty>` — oldest unread slot as
243	  `&T`.
244	- `ReadSlot::release(self)` — frees the slot
245	  (`consumer_idx + 1`, `Release`). Dropping without release
246	  leaves the slot unread — the next reserve_slot returns it
247	  again.
248	
249	Each side reserves at most **one slot at a time**: the guard
250	holds the endpoint's `&mut` borrow, so a second `reserve_slot`
251	while a guard is live is a compile error, not a runtime state.
252	
253	Both endpoints also expose `user() -> &[AtomicU32; 16]` — the
254	header's app-owned scratch line; see
255	[Blocking and user words](#blocking-and-user-words).
256	
257	Guard internals: both guards hold **raw slot pointers** and
258	mint `&T` / `&mut T` per access via Deref. A reference *field*
259	would be argument-protected (noalias) for the entire
```
