```
259	would be argument-protected (noalias) for the entire
260	`commit`/`release` call, but the store inside that call hands
261	the slot to the other side, which may access it while the
262	protector is still live — an aliasing race Miri flags
263	(found in 0.3.0-6 by the threaded test).
264	
265	Batch release (resolves the batching question): deferred —
266	the guard API leaves room for a `release_n(n)` later; the
267	single-slot guard is the whole surface this cycle.
268	
269	## Blocking and user words
270	
271	The crate never blocks and never spins. `reserve_slot`
272	returning [`Full`]/[`Empty`] immediately is the entire API;
273	what to do about it — spin, yield, park, await — is policy,
274	and policy belongs to the layer above (an embedded caller may
275	WFE on an interrupt, an async runtime wants a waker, a pinned
276	busy-poll thread wants a pure spin; no single crate-blessed
277	hybrid fits all three).
278	
279	What the crate provides is mechanism: the header's `user`
280	line — 16 `AtomicU32` words, zeroed by `Ring::init`, exposed
281	via `user()` on both endpoints, and never read, written, or
282	interpreted by the crate afterwards. It is the well-known
283	shared-memory home where independently written peers can
284	build a wakeup protocol (waiter flags, futex words, sequence
285	numbers). `AtomicU32` deliberately: futex-shaped, portable to
286	targets without 64-bit atomics, race-free under concurrent
287	peer access.
288	
289	Contracts and cautions for that layer:
290	
291	- **Lost-wakeup discipline** — the sleep side must re-check
292	  the ring *after* publishing its "wake me" flag and before
293	  sleeping (`set flag → reserve_slot once more → sleep`), and
294	  the wake side checks the flag *after* its commit/release
295	  (`commit → check flag → wake`). Skipping the re-check races
296	  a commit landing between flag-set and sleep: the waker saw
297	  no flag, the sleeper saw no message — it sleeps forever.
298	  futex-style primitives exist precisely to make the final
299	  check-and-sleep atomic.
300	- **Values, not addresses** — a pointer stored in the region
301	  is meaningless in the peer's address space (and a
302	  dereference of peer-writable bytes besides). Store data;
303	  keep pointers on your side of the wall.
304	- **Timeouts** — a peer can die while you sleep; every wait
305	  should carry a timeout so a dead producer degrades the
306	  consumer to periodic polling, not a hang.
307	
308	## Validation
309	
310	Per-commit, alongside the cargo cycle:
311	
312	- `cargo +nightly miri test` — the full suite, threaded test
313	  included (its message count is reduced under `cfg(miri)`;
314	  interpreted spin loops are slow). Miri has caught two real
315	  Stacked Borrows violations: the init retag (0.3.0-4) and
316	  the guard argument-protector race (0.3.0-6). The latter
317	  reproduces only when the threaded test runs, so the full
318	  suite — not a non-threaded subset — stays in the ladder.
319	- `indices_survive_u32_wrap` pins the free-running-index wrap
320	  argument (fill/drain across `u32::MAX` with masked
321	  positions intact).
322	- [loom](https://docs.rs/loom) for exhaustive ordering
323	  exploration is a possible follow-on (Ideas).
324	
325	## Messaging layer: pools and descriptor queues
326	
327	Design for the layer above the ring. The pool half is
328	as-built as of the 0.6.0 cycle (`src/pool.rs`: layout,
329	init/attach, alloc/free); descriptor queues and provenance
330	remain design. The sections above are the as-built record of
331	the ring itself.
332	
333	The ring's `reserve_slot` fuses three acts that a messaging
334	system needs separated: it allocates message memory (the slot),
335	assigns queue position (the reservation is the ring head), and
336	opens a publication window (the guard exclusively borrows the
337	endpoint, blocking all other sends). Getting a message must not
338	imply sending it now — or ever. The fix is a layer, not a ring
339	rework: pools own message memory, queues carry small
340	descriptors, and the existing ring is the unchanged primitive
341	underneath (reserve-to-commit becomes a momentary act, where
342	the fusion is harmless).
343	
344	### System model (overview)
345	
346	The layer's rules at a glance; each bullet links to the
347	section that expands it.
348	
```

id: toolu_01MGMYWrRHHrTJ1eiW1Lke88
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/notes/bugs.md",
  "old_string": "1. `reserve_slot` (both `Consumer` and `Producer`) is a strictly\n   dominated API rung — `reserve_slot_with` does everything it does,\n   never worse and sometimes better — and its naive \"loop on the\n   fallible call\" usage carries a measurable latency + jitter cost.\n   Consider removing both.\n   - **What we measured.** iiac-perf's zcr round-trip benches\n     (`-d 60`, unpinned 3900X, one message in flight) compare the\n     tiers under real cross-core traffic. `zcr-raw-2t` (main loops on\n     `reserve_slot` while waiting for the reply) lands a body mean\n     ~130 ns, multimodal across ~120–150 ns, trimmed `min..n2` stdev\n     ~15 ns. `zcr-with-2t` / `zcr-spin-2t` (same wait via\n     `reserve_slot_with` / `reserve_slot_spin`) land ~110 ns, body\n     clustered at 100/109/110 ns, trimmed stdev ~7 ns. So the raw\n     retry pattern costs ~+30 ns and ~2× the body jitter. `with` and\n     `spin` are statistically identical, as expected —\n     `reserve_slot_spin` *is* `reserve_slot_with(policy::spin)`.\n   - **Why (we think).** The only code difference on the wait path is\n     that `reserve_slot`-in-a-loop re-reads the caller-*owned* index\n     (`consumer_idx` on the read side, `producer_idx` on the write\n     side) on every iteration, because each call reloads it;\n     `reserve_slot_with` loads the owned index once and re-reads only\n     the peer's index per attempt (its doc already states this).\n     A relaxed atomic load can't be hoisted/coalesced out of a loop\n     by LLVM, so the naive retry genuinely issues the extra load each\n     spin. We think that lengthens and de-tightens the spin, so it\n     samples the peer's store later and less regularly — a higher\n     mean and a multimodal body in ~10 ns steps (≈ one Zen 2\n     cross-core coherence hop). The two indices are each\n     `CacheAligned` on separate lines, so this is **not** false\n     sharing between them.\n   - **Current thinking.** `reserve_slot` ≡\n     `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)` (give up after the first failed\n     attempt) — identical loads, identical result, and the `|_| false`\n     loop folds away (worth a disasm confirm). For any retry beyond\n     one probe, `_with` hoists the owned load and wins. The desirable\n     always-non-blocking mode (e.g. driving the ring from a Tokio\n     task) is `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)`; the better hybrid\n     (\"spin a bounded N, then return pending and let the runtime\n     park\") is `reserve_slot_with(|attempt| attempt < N)` — both\n     things `reserve_slot` can't express. So the proposed shape is a\n     two-rung ladder per endpoint: `reserve_slot_with` (fallible\n     primitive) + `reserve_slot_spin` (forever-spin convenience),\n     dropping `reserve_slot`. If a real consumer later makes\n     `_with(|_| false)` common, add a thin `try_reserve_slot` alias\n     (named like `Sender::try_send`) then — not before (no consumers\n     exist yet).\n   - **Downstream.** iiac-perf's `zcr-raw-1t` / `zcr-raw-2t` call\n     `reserve_slot` directly and only exist to measure this naive\n     pattern; removing the method drops (or relabels) those benches.\n     Tracked on the iiac-perf side.",
  "new_string": "_No known bugs._"
}

> AGENT

Diff is coherent — net −74 lines, `## Bugs` now reads `_No known bugs._`. Here's the commit description for your approval.

**Title:**
```
refactor: drop reserve_slot, keep _with ladder
```

**Body:**
```
`reserve_slot` was strictly dominated by
`reserve_slot_with(|_| false)` (identical loads, folds to the
same code), and its loop-on-fallible usage cost a measured
+30 ns / ~2x body jitter under cross-core traffic (bug #1).
Drop it for the two-rung ladder: `reserve_slot_with` (fallible
primitive) + `reserve_slot_spin` (forever-spin convenience).

- Cargo.toml: 0.7.3 -> 0.8.0
- src/consumer.rs: delete reserve_slot; reserve_slot_with is
  now the self-contained primitive (absorbs the |_| false
  single-probe note + raw-pointer rationale); repoint Empty,
  struct, reserve_slot_spin, and ReadSlot doc links
- src/producer.rs: same for the write side (Full, WriteSlot,
  the >=-corruption rationale)

Both backfills done. That adds two more touches to `chores-01.md`, so let me update the commit body's chores bullet accordingly. Here's the **final description**:

**Title:**
```
refactor: drop reserve_slot, keep _with ladder
```

**Body:**
```
`reserve_slot` was strictly dominated by
`reserve_slot_with(|_| false)` (identical loads, folds to the
same code), and its loop-on-fallible usage cost a measured
+30 ns / ~2x body jitter under cross-core traffic (bug #1).
Drop it for the two-rung ladder: `reserve_slot_with` (fallible
primitive) + `reserve_slot_spin` (forever-spin convenience).

- Cargo.toml: 0.7.3 -> 0.8.0
- src/consumer.rs: delete reserve_slot; reserve_slot_with is
  now the self-contained primitive (absorbs the |_| false
  single-probe note + raw-pointer rationale); repoint Empty,
  struct, reserve_slot_spin, and ReadSlot doc links
- src/producer.rs: same for the write side (Full, WriteSlot,
  the >=-corruption rationale)
- src/lib.rs: check_type doc; test single-probes ->
  _with(|_| false); threaded_spsc loops on _with(|_| false)
- src/registry.rs: test spin-loops -> reserve_slot_spin; drop
  now-unused Empty/Full imports
- src/pool.rs: alloc doc cross-reference
- src/bin/zc-ring-x1-demo.rs: _1t -> _with(|_| false), _2t ->
  reserve_slot_spin; module doc
- examples/readme.rs: _with(|_| false)
- README.md: API line + four snippets
- notes/bugs.md: remove fixed bug #1
- notes/todo.md: Done entry [[16]]; two incidental mentions
- notes/chores/chores-01.md: new chores section + blocking-intro
  reconciliation subsection; backfill seam-lines Commits: [[31]];
  add the missing section for 35f144b
- notes/ring-buffer-design.md: API section -> two-rung ladder;
  reconcile the "never spins" blocking intro

BREAKING-CHANGE: Consumer/Producer::reserve_slot removed; use
reserve_slot_with(|_| false) or reserve_slot_spin.
```

```
vc-x1 0.67.0

Dual-repo commit+push+finalize in one resumable command.

Collapses today's manual Commit-Push-Finalize Flow into a
single subcommand with two interactive approval gates and a
state machine with persistent progress so interruptions can
resume without re-doing completed stages.

Stages: preflight (fmt/clippy/test) → review (approve diff)
→ message ($EDITOR / --title+--body, approve text) →
commit-app → commit-claude (skipped if clean) → bookmark-both
→ push-app → finalize-claude. Failures in commit-app /
commit-claude / bookmark-both roll both repos back via
`jj op restore` to the snapshot recorded before commit-app.
After push-app succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and
recovery is forward-only.

Non-interactive use: pass both --title and --body plus --yes
to skip the review gate. Saved state carries title/body
across resumes so only the first invocation needs them.

Usage: vc-x1 push [OPTIONS] [BOOKMARK]

Arguments:
  [BOOKMARK]
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (positional form of `--bookmark`).
          
          Accepting a positional lets the common case read as `vc-x1 push main`
          without the `--bookmark` ceremony; `--bookmark` is kept as an alias
          for scripts and for composition with other args. The two forms
          conflict if both supplied.

Options:
      --bookmark <BOOKMARK>
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (flag form; see positional)

  -V, --version
          Print the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner as the first line, then continue. With
          no subcommand, prints the banner and exits.
          
          Replaces clap's auto-version (which would exit after printing): the
          banner now rides along with normal subcommand execution rather than
          gating it, so scripts can capture the version *and* the command's
          output in one invocation.

      --restart
          Clear any saved state file and start from stage 1

  -v, --verbose...
          Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace

      --from <STAGE>
          Explicit stage to jump to (advanced / debug use)

          Possible values:
          - preflight:       Run fmt / clippy / test / install / retest
          - review:          Present diff for the first approval gate
          - message:         Compose / edit the commit message; present for
            second gate
          - commit-app:      Commit the app repo
          - commit-claude:   Commit the `.claude` session repo (skipped if
            empty)
          - bookmark-both:   Advance both bookmarks to `@-`
          - push-app:        `jj git push --bookmark <b> -R .`
          - finalize-claude: `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <b>
            ...`

      --log <LOG>
          Log file path (captures all levels)

      --step
          Pause between every stage for an interactive approval gate

      --status
          Print where the saved state thinks we are and exit

      --recheck
          Re-run `preflight` even on resume (default: skip if last run
          succeeded)
```

      --title <STR>

  "content": "`reserve_slot` was strictly dominated by\n`reserve_slot_with(|_| false)` (identical loads, folds to the\nsame code), and its loop-on-fallible usage cost a measured\n+30 ns / ~2x body jitter under cross-core traffic (bug #1).\nDrop it for the two-rung ladder: `reserve_slot_with` (fallible\nprimitive) + `reserve_slot_spin` (forever-spin convenience).\n\n- Cargo.toml: 0.7.3 -> 0.8.0\n- src/consumer.rs: delete reserve_slot; reserve_slot_with is\n  now the self-contained primitive (absorbs the |_| false\n  single-probe note + raw-pointer rationale); repoint Empty,\n  struct, reserve_slot_spin, and ReadSlot doc links\n- src/producer.rs: same for the write side (Full, WriteSlot,\n  the >=-corruption rationale)\n- src/lib.rs: check_type doc; test single-probes ->\n  _with(|_| false); threaded_spsc loops on _with(|_| false)\n- src/registry.rs: test spin-loops -> reserve_slot_spin; drop\n  now-unused Empty/Full imports\n- src/pool.rs: alloc doc cross-reference\n- src/bin/zc-ring-x1-demo.rs: _1t -> _with(|_| false), _2t ->\n  reserve_slot_spin; module doc\n- examples/readme.rs: _with(|_| false)\n- README.md: API line + four snippets\n- notes/bugs.md: remove fixed bug #1\n- notes/todo.md: Done entry [[16]]; two incidental mentions\n- notes/chores/chores-01.md: new chores section + blocking-intro\n  reconciliation subsection; backfill seam-lines [[31]] and add +\n  backfill the 35f144b section [[32]] (typo in its title kept to\n  match the commit)\n- notes/ring-buffer-design.md: API section -> two-rung ladder;\n  reconcile the \"never spins\" blocking intro\n\nBREAKING-CHANGE: Consumer/Producer::reserve_slot removed; use\nreserve_slot_with(|_| false) or reserve_slot_spin.\n"
}

1	//! Shipped wait policies — the models users copy.
2	//!
3	//! A wait policy is any `FnMut(u32) -> bool`: the `_with`
4	//! methods ([`Pool::alloc_with`](crate::Pool::alloc_with),
5	//! [`Producer::reserve_slot_with`](crate::Producer::reserve_slot_with),
6	//! [`Consumer::reserve_slot_with`](crate::Consumer::reserve_slot_with))
7	//! call it after each failed attempt with the attempt count
8	//! (0-based, saturating); returning `false` gives up and the
9	//! operation returns its usual error.
10	//!
11	//! - The crate ships only the policies its own demo and
12	//!   tests use; compose your own (bounded retries, deadline,
13	//!   spin-then-yield) as capturing closures at the call
14	//!   site, or bake one into your own wrapper method.
15	//! - Policies are polling strategies. True blocking (futex,
16	//!   eventfd, async wakers) needs a peer wake over the
17	//!   header's user line and is a layer above this crate.
18	
19	/// Spin forever: hint the CPU and keep waiting.
20	///
21	/// `#[inline]`: non-generic, so without the hint a
22	/// monomorphized `_with` in another crate calls it out of
23	/// line — measurable on the demo's ~3 ns loops.
24	#[inline]
25	pub fn spin(_attempt: u32) -> bool {
26	    core::hint::spin_loop();
27	    true
28	}

292	/// spsc_ring_one_msg_1t through `reserve_slot_spin` — the
293	/// shipped-model form of the three-way seam check; like the
294	/// raw 1t it never actually waits.
295	fn spsc_ring_one_msg_1t_spin() -> f64 {
296	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
297	    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)
298	        .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots
299	        .split();
300	
301	    let start = Instant::now();
302	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
303	        s.spawn(move || {
304	            pin_to_cpu(0);
305	            for i in 0..COUNT {
306	                let mut slot = producer.reserve_slot_spin::<Msg>();
307	                slot.seq = i;
308	                slot.commit();
309	                let msg = consumer.reserve_slot_spin::<Msg>();
310	                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
311	                msg.release();
312	            }
313	        });
314	    });
315	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
316	}
317	
318	/// spsc_ring_one_msg_2t through `reserve_slot_with` with a
319	/// spinning closure — the closure form of the three-way seam
320	/// check under real waiting.
321	fn spsc_ring_one_msg_2t_closure(pin: PinPair) -> f64 {
322	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
323	    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)
324	        .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots
325	        .split();
326	
327	    let start = Instant::now();
328	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
329	        s.spawn(move || {
330	            if let Some((p, _)) = pin {
331	                pin_to_cpu(p);
332	            }
333	            for i in 0..COUNT {
334	                let mut slot = producer
335	                    .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
336	                        std::hint::spin_loop();
337	                        true
338	                    })
339	                    .unwrap(); // OK: the closure never gives up
340	                slot.seq = i;
341	                slot.commit();
342	            }
343	        });
344	        s.spawn(move || {
345	            if let Some((_, c)) = pin {
346	                pin_to_cpu(c);
347	            }
348	            for i in 0..COUNT {
349	                let msg = consumer
350	                    .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {

352	                        true
353	                    })
354	                    .unwrap(); // OK: the closure never gives up
355	                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
356	                msg.release();
357	            }
358	        });
359	    });
360	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
361	}

297	    /// [`alloc`](Pool::alloc), spinning until a buffer is
298	    /// available — [`alloc_with`](Pool::alloc_with) under the
299	    /// never-quitting [`policy::spin`](crate::policy::spin),
300	    /// so no `Result`.
301	    pub fn alloc_spin<T>(&mut self) -> BufSlot<'a, T>
302	    where
303	        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,
304	    {
305	        match self.alloc_with(crate::policy::spin) {
306	            Ok(buf_slot) => buf_slot,
307	            Err(Exhausted) => unreachable!("policy::spin never gives up"),
308	        }
309	    }

915	    #[test]
916	    fn alloc_with_policy_counts_and_gives_up() {
917	        let mut r = Region::new();
918	        let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut r.0, TEST_CACHE_LINE_SIZE, 4).unwrap();
919	        // Drain the pool so alloc_with must wait.
920	        let held: [_; 4] = core::array::from_fn(|_| pool.alloc::<Msg>().unwrap());
921	
922	        // The policy sees 0-based attempt counts and its
923	        // `false` becomes Err(Exhausted).
924	        let mut seen = Vec::new();
925	        let err = pool
926	            .alloc_with::<Msg>(|attempt| {
927	                seen.push(attempt);
928	                attempt < 2
929	            })
930	            .err()
931	            .unwrap();
932	        assert_eq!(err, Exhausted);
933	        assert_eq!(seen, [0, 1, 2]);
934	
935	        // A freed buffer ends the wait with Ok.
936	        let mut freed = false;
937	        let mut held = held.into_iter();
938	        let buf_slot = pool
939	            .alloc_with::<Msg>(|_| {
940	                if !freed {
941	                    held.next().unwrap().free();
942	                    freed = true;
943	                }
944	                true
945	            })
946	            .unwrap();
947	        buf_slot.free();
948	        held.for_each(BufSlot::free);
949	    }
950	
951	    #[test]

1	//! Demo binary: both primitives working across threads,
2	//! with throughput printed — run `cargo run --release`, or
3	//! `cargo install --path . --locked` and run
4	//! `zc-ring-x1-demo`. `-V`/`--version` prints the
5	//! version-of-record so you know exactly which build you
6	//! are testing.
7	//!
8	//! - Part 1, the ring: an SPSC pair moves typed messages
9	//!   in place (reserve → write → commit; reserve → read →
10	//!   release) — first both ends on one thread
11	//!   (the ring's own cost), then one producer thread to
12	//!   one consumer thread: unpinned, pinned to two different
13	//!   physical cores, and pinned to one physical core's two
14	//!   SMT siblings (shared L1/L2, when the CPU has SMT).
15	//! - Part 2, the pool: an allocator thread allocs and
16	//!   fills `BufSlot`s and hands them to a freer thread —
17	//!   "send" today is moving the guard (see the README's
18	//!   usage model); getting a buffer implies nothing about
19	//!   when it is sent or freed.
20	//! - The composed form — descriptors through the ring,
21	//!   payloads at rest in pool buffers — runs between them:
22	//!   alloc → into_desc → ring → resolve → free, with the
23	//!   same placement ladder as the raw ring.
24	//! - The `_closure` / `_spin` lines are the wait-policy
25	//!   seam exemplar: one workload written in all three forms
26	//!   side by side — raw (the hand-rolled wait loop, the
27	//!   fastest form we know for the shape and the baseline
28	//!   the others are measured against), `_closure` (a
29	//!   spinning closure through the `reserve_slot_with`
30	//!   hook), and `_spin` (the same hook under the shipped
31	//!   `policy::spin`). Calibrated
32	//!   measurement lives in iiac-perf; these lines are the
33	//!   eyeball check.
34	
```

248	/// spsc_ring_one_msg_1t through `reserve_slot_with` — the
249	/// closure form of the wait-policy seam check, measured
250	/// against the base `spsc_ring_one_msg_1t` line above it.
251	///
252	/// The ring never blocks in this loop, so the policy asserts
253	/// instead of waiting and the line measures the probe path's
254	/// pure overhead — at ~3 ns/msg any per-call cost shows as a
255	/// visible percentage.
256	fn spsc_ring_one_msg_1t_closure() -> f64 {
257	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
258	    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)
259	        .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots
260	        .split();
261	
262	    let start = Instant::now();
263	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
264	        s.spawn(move || {
265	            pin_to_cpu(0);
266	            for i in 0..COUNT {
267	                let mut slot = producer
268	                    .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
269	                        panic!("spsc_ring_one_msg_1t_closure: producer Full SHOULD NOT HAPPEN")
270	                    })
271	                    .unwrap(); // OK: the policy panics instead of giving up
272	                slot.seq = i;
273	                slot.commit();
274	                let msg = consumer
275	                    .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
276	                        panic!("spsc_ring_one_msg_1t_closure: consumer Empty SHOULD NOT HAPPEN")
277	                    })
278	                    .unwrap(); // OK: the policy panics instead of giving up
279	                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
280	                msg.release();
281	            }
282	        });
283	    });
284	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
285	}
286	
287	/// spsc_ring_one_msg_2t through `reserve_slot_with` with a
288	/// spinning closure — the closure form of the wait-policy seam
289	/// check under real waiting, measured against the base
290	/// `spsc_ring_one_msg_2t` line (the shipped `policy::spin`).
291	fn spsc_ring_one_msg_2t_closure(pin: PinPair) -> f64 {
292	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
293	    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)
294	        .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots
295	        .split();
296	
297	    let start = Instant::now();
298	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
299	        s.spawn(move || {
300	            if let Some((p, _)) = pin {
301	                pin_to_cpu(p);
302	            }
303	            for i in 0..COUNT {
304	                let mut slot = producer
305	                    .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
306	                        std::hint::spin_loop();
307	                        true
308	                    })
309	                    .unwrap(); // OK: the closure never gives up
310	                slot.seq = i;
311	                slot.commit();
312	            }
313	        });
314	        s.spawn(move || {
315	            if let Some((_, c)) = pin {
316	                pin_to_cpu(c);
317	            }
318	            for i in 0..COUNT {
319	                let msg = consumer
320	                    .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
321	                        std::hint::spin_loop();
322	                        true
323	                    })
324	                    .unwrap(); // OK: the closure never gives up
325	                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
326	                msg.release();
327	            }
328	        });
329	    });
```

```
226	Producer — reserve_slot_with/commit, in place:
227	
228	- `producer.reserve_slot_with::<T>(on_full) ->
229	  Result<WriteSlot<'_, T>, Full>` — next free slot as `&mut T`
230	  (zerocopy `FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout`), retrying
231	  under the injected wait policy — `on_full(attempt) -> bool`,
232	  `|_| false` for a single non-blocking probe. `T` geometry is
233	  asserted against the slot size on each call (a mismatch is a
234	  programming error, so it panics; typed endpoints that check
235	  once are a follow-on — see [todo.md](todo.md)).
236	- `producer.reserve_slot_spin::<T>() -> WriteSlot<'_, T>` — the
237	  forever-spin convenience (`reserve_slot_with` under
238	  `policy::spin`), so no `Result`.
239	- `WriteSlot::commit(self)` — publishes the slot
240	  (`producer_idx + 1`, `Release`). Dropping without commit
241	  abandons the reservation (nothing published).
242	
243	Consumer — reserve_slot_with/release, in place:
244	
245	- `consumer.reserve_slot_with::<T>(on_empty) ->
246	  Result<ReadSlot<'_, T>, Empty>` — oldest unread slot as
247	  `&T`, retrying under the injected wait policy (`|_| false`
248	  for a single non-blocking probe).
249	- `consumer.reserve_slot_spin::<T>() -> ReadSlot<'_, T>` — the
250	  forever-spin convenience, so no `Result`.
251	- `ReadSlot::release(self)` — frees the slot
252	  (`consumer_idx + 1`, `Release`). Dropping without release
253	  leaves the slot unread — the next reservation returns it
254	  again.
255	
256	Each side reserves at most **one slot at a time**: the guard
257	holds the endpoint's `&mut` borrow, so a second reservation
258	while a guard is live is a compile error, not a runtime state.
259	
260	Both endpoints also expose `user() -> &[AtomicU32; 16]` — the
261	header's app-owned scratch line; see
262	[Blocking and user words](#blocking-and-user-words).
263	
264	Guard internals: both guards hold **raw slot pointers** and
265	mint `&T` / `&mut T` per access via Deref. A reference *field*
266	would be argument-protected (noalias) for the entire
267	`commit`/`release` call, but the store inside that call hands
268	the slot to the other side, which may access it while the
269	protector is still live — an aliasing race Miri flags
270	(found in 0.3.0-6 by the threaded test).
271	
272	Batch release (resolves the batching question): deferred —
273	the guard API leaves room for a `release_n(n)` later; the
274	single-slot guard is the whole surface this cycle.
275	
276	## Blocking and user words
277	
278	The ring's fallible core never blocks: `reserve_slot_with`
279	returning [`Full`]/[`Empty`] under a give-up policy (`|_| false`)
280	is the primitive. *How* to wait on it — spin, yield, park,
281	await — is injected policy: a shipped [`policy::spin`] drives
282	the [`reserve_slot_spin`] convenience, but anything that
283	actually sleeps belongs to the layer above (an embedded caller
284	may WFE on an interrupt, an async runtime wants a waker, a
285	pinned busy-poll thread wants a pure spin; no single
```

```
575	rung: `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)` does everything it did
576	with identical loads, and its naive loop-on-the-fallible-call
577	usage carried a measured +30 ns / ~2× body jitter under
578	cross-core traffic (bug #1, whose raw-pattern benches live in
579	iiac-perf's `zcr-raw-*`). Removing it leaves a two-rung ladder
580	per endpoint — `reserve_slot_with` (fallible primitive) +
581	`reserve_slot_spin` (forever-spin convenience).
582	
583	- `reserve_slot_with` is now the self-contained primitive: its
584	  doc absorbed the single-probe (`|_| false`) note and the
585	  raw-pointer / `>=`-corruption rationale the deleted method
586	  used to host.
587	- Call sites split by intent: single-probe (the `_1t` demos,
588	  the assertion tests, the README examples) →
589	  `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)`; loop-on-fallible spin waits
590	  (the `_2t` demos, the registry tests) → `reserve_slot_spin`.
591	- `threaded_spsc` keeps looping on `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)`
592	  so the fallible primitive still has a threaded-contention
593	  test distinct from `threaded_spsc_spin_variants` (which
594	  covers the spin rung).
595	- No `try_reserve_slot` alias yet: nothing outside tests makes
596	  `_with(|_| false)` common, so per bug #1 the alias waits for
597	  a real consumer.
598	
599	### ring-buffer-design.md: blocking intro reconciled
600	
601	The `## Blocking and user words` intro still read "the crate
602	never blocks and never spins" — accurate before the
603	wait-policy hook, but `reserve_slot_spin` spins now, so a bare
604	method rename would have left an inline contradiction. The
605	intro was reframed instead: the ring's fallible core never
606	blocks, and *how* to wait is injected policy (a shipped
607	`policy::spin` drives `reserve_slot_spin`); anything that
608	actually sleeps still belongs to the layer above via the
609	`user()` words. We think the rest of that section (the
610	`user()` wakeup-protocol contract) is unaffected by the API
611	change.
612	
613	### As-built ladder
614	
615	- `0.8.0` refactor: drop reserve_slot, keep _with ladder
616

618

They\ncarry their weight only if callers reach for spin often; no\nconsumer outside the demo and tests does, so they go, leaving\none rung per operation (`*_with` + a shipped `policy::spin`\npolicy). `policy::spin` stays public — it is the migration\ntarget and what the base demo lines now pass.\n\n- Spin call sites migrate to `*_with(policy::spin).unwrap()`\n  (`// OK: policy::spin never gives up` on non-test sites):\n  the base 2t demos, the registry / lib / pool tests.\n- `Pool::alloc_spin` removed for symmetry (todo named only\n  `reserve_slot_spin`, but the same rationale applies); its\n  lone test became `alloc_with_spin_returns_ok`.\n\n### demo: the three-way wait-policy seam collapses\n\nThe demo carried a `raw` / `_closure` / `_spin` seam exemplar\n— one workload in three wait forms, side by side, as an\neyeball zero-cost check. Here's the **commit description** for your approval:

**Title:**
```
refactor: drop reserve_slot_spin and alloc_spin
```

**Body:**
```
`reserve_slot_spin` and the pool's `alloc_spin` were the last
`_spin` conveniences — three-line wrappers over
`*_with(policy::spin)` that hid the always-`Ok` unwrap. No
consumer outside the demo and tests reaches for spin, so they
go, leaving one rung per operation (`*_with` + the shipped
`policy::spin`, still public).

- Cargo.toml: 0.8.0 -> 0.9.0
- src/consumer.rs: delete Consumer::reserve_slot_spin
- src/producer.rs: delete Producer::reserve_slot_spin
- src/pool.rs: delete Pool::alloc_spin; its test becomes
  alloc_with_spin_returns_ok via alloc_with(policy::spin)
- src/registry.rs: test spin sites ->
  reserve_slot_with(policy::spin).unwrap()
- src/lib.rs: threaded_spsc_spin_variants ->
  reserve_slot_with(policy::spin)
- src/bin/zc-ring-x1-demo.rs: drop the four seam fns
  (_1t_spin, _2t_spin, _1t_closure, _2t_closure), their call
  sites, and the three-way seam narrative; base 2t lines pass
  policy::spin (-210 lines)
- README.md: drop the reserve_slot_spin prose + the
  zcr-spin-1t/2t benchmark blocks (zcr-raw/with kept)
- notes/ring-buffer-design.md: drop the reserve_slot_spin API
  bullets and the blocking-intro mention
- notes/todo.md: Done entry [[17]]; remove the picked-up
  "Remove reserve_slot_spin"; renumber
- notes/chores/chores-01.md: chores section (spin removal,
  demo-seam collapse, README benches) + backfill the 0.8.0
  section [[33]]

BREAKING-CHANGE: Consumer/Producer::reserve_slot_spin and
Pool::alloc_spin removed; use reserve_slot_with(policy::spin)
/ alloc_with(policy::spin).
```
